Algorithm FGC produces the GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
apart
from that,
for every equivalence class
\vartheta\in\Theta\langle E,F\rangle
,
Algorithm FGC writes the unique name for
\vartheta

[
5
]
.
By Statement
4.46
,

\mathrm{size}(R\langle E,F\rangle)\leq 2\cdot|W|\leq 2n
.
Then by Proposition
2.2
,
the time-complexity of inserting the elements
of
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
into an empty red-black tree, not allowing duplicate values in the resulting red-black tree

RBT1
, is
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
,

We now show Statement 3.
In the relation
BSTEP\langle E,F\rangle\subseteq C\langle E,F\rangle\times C\langle E,F\rangle
, we collect all pairs
({b}_{i},{b})
such that there is a rewrite rule

\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}
in
R\langle E,F\rangle
.
By Statement
4.46
,
|BSTEP\langle E,F\rangle|\leq 2n
.
Then by Proposition
2.2
,
the time-complexity of inserting the elements
of
BSTEP\langle E,F\rangle
into an empty red-black tree, not allowing duplicate values in the resulting red-black tree, is
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
.
∎

We give the GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
as input to an algorithm by giving the red-black tree
RBT1

output by Algorithm FGC storing the GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
.
We now define an algorithm deciding whether
R\langle E,F\rangle
is total.

Definition 4.49
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
.

Algorithm Deciding whether GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
is Total (DTOT for short).

Input: The signature
\Sigma
, the Boolean value
FLAG\langle E,F\rangle
, the set of constants
C\langle E,F\rangle
,
and the red-black tree
RBT
storing the GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
.

Output: Boolean.
{
\mathrm{true}
if
R\langle E,F\rangle
total. Otherwise
\mathrm{false}
. }

var

i
,
m,count:{\mathbb{N}}
;

If
FLAG\langle E,F\rangle=\mathrm{false}
, then return
\mathrm{false}
. If
R\langle E,F\rangle=\emptyset
, then return
\mathrm{false}
.
Otherwise, traverse the red-black tree
RBT
in in-order traversal, and read all binary sequences stored in the red-black tree
RBT

in the lexicographic order
<_{lex}
on binary sequences.
For every
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\sigma\in\Sigma\langle R\langle E,F\rangle_{m}
, read the rules with
\sigma
appering on the left-hand side
one after another.
During this process,

⬇

for every

\sigma\in\Sigma\langle E,F\rangle

do

begin

count all rules with
\sigma
appearing on the left-hand side, and store the result in the variable
count
;

m:=\mathrm{arity}(\sigma)
;

{\bf if}\;count<|C\langle E,F\rangle|^{m}

then

return

\mathrm{false}

end

{\bf output}(\mathrm{true})

Lemma 4.50
.

Algorithm DTOT decides in
O(n)
time whether
R\langle E,F\rangle
is total, where
n=\mathrm{size}(E)+\mathrm{size}(F)
.

Proof.

If
R\langle E,F\rangle=\emptyset
, then by Statement
4.40
, we are done.

Assume that
R\langle E,F\rangle\neq\emptyset
. If
FLAG\langle E,F\rangle=\mathrm{false}
, that is,
|\Sigma\langle E,F\rangle|<|\Sigma|
,
then

R\langle E,F\rangle
is not total.
Assume that
FLAG\langle E,F\rangle=\mathrm{true}
, that is,
|\Sigma\langle E,F\rangle|=|\Sigma|
. Then for each symbol
\sigma\in\Sigma
,
we count the rewrite rules in
R\langle E,F\rangle
with
\sigma
appearing in the left-hand side and store the result in the variable
count
.
If there exists a symbol
\sigma\in\Sigma_{m}
,
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
such that for
\sigma
,
count<|\langle C\langle E,F\rangle\rangle|^{m}
,
then there exist constants
{b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
such that
there does not exist a rewrite rule with left-hand side

\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})

in
R\langle E,F\rangle
.
Therefore,
R\langle E,F\rangle
is not total.
Conversely if
R\langle E,F\rangle
is not total, then there exists a symbol
\sigma\in\Sigma_{m}
and constants
{b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
such that
there does not exist a rewrite rule with left-hand side
\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})

in
R\langle E,F\rangle
. Then for
\sigma
,
count<|\langle C\langle E,F\rangle\rangle|^{m}
.
Consequently if
R\langle E,F\rangle
is total, then Algorithm DTOT outputs true; otherwise it returns false.

We now consider the time complexity of the Algorithm DTOT.
If
|\Sigma\langle E,F\rangle|<|\Sigma|
or
R\langle E,F\rangle=\emptyset
, then Algorithm DTOT takes less than
O(n)
time.
Assume that
|\Sigma\langle E,F\rangle|=|\Sigma|
and
R\langle E,F\rangle\neq\emptyset
.
Then
Algorithm DTOT traverse the red-black tree
RBT
in in-order traversal, and it reads all binary sequences stored in the red-black tree
RBT

in the lexicographic order
<_{lex}
on binary sequences. For every
\sigma\in\Sigma\langle R\langle E,F\rangle\rangle
, it reads the rules with
\sigma
appering on the left-hand side
one after another, and
counts the rewrite rules in
R\langle E,F\rangle
with
\sigma
appearing in the left-hand side for all symbols
\sigma\in\Sigma
. This takes
O(n)
time.

If there exists a symbol
\sigma\in\Sigma
such that
\sigma
does not appear in
R\langle E,F\rangle
, then Algorithm DTOT outpouts false, and
does not evaluate the expression
count<|C\langle E,F\rangle|^{m}
.
Otherwise, for every symbol
\sigma\in\Sigma_{m}
,
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we carry out
m
multiplications when computing
|C\langle E,F\rangle|^{m}
.
Because each symbol

\sigma\in\Sigma\langle R\langle E,F\rangle\rangle_{m}
,
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
, has
m
constants in its arguments in the left-hand side of some rewrite rule in

R\langle E,F\rangle
.
The number of multiplications is less than or equal to

\sum_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma\langle R\langle E,F\rangle\rangle_{m}}m\leq

\mathrm{size}(R\langle E,F\rangle)\leq 2n

by Statement
4.46
.
Consequently, the number of multiplications is less than or equal to
2n
.
The number of comparisons of nonnegative integers is
|\Sigma|\leq n
.
Therefore the time complexity of algorithm DTOT is

O(n)
.
∎

5
Running Examples Continued

We now illustrate the concepts of Section
4
by continuing our running examples.
We compute the sets of constants
C\langle E,F\rangle
,

C\langle F,E\rangle
,
C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, and the GTRSs
R\langle E,F\rangle
,
R\langle F,E\rangle
,

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.

Recall that
E
and
F
are GTESs over a
signature
\Sigma
, and
W
stands for

ST\langle E\cup F\rangle
.

.

We continue Example

.

C\langle E,F\rangle=\{\,\{\,\#\,\}
,
\{\,\$\,\}
,
\{\,f(\#),\;g(\#)\,\}
,

\{\,f(\$)\,\}
,
\{\,g(\$)\,\}\,\}
.

R\langle E,F\rangle=\{\,\#\dot{\rightarrow}\#/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle},\;\$\dot{\rightarrow}\$/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle},\;\newline
f(\#/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}f(\#)/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle},\;f(\$/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}f(\$)/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle},\;\newline

.

.

.

C\langle F,E\rangle=\{\,\{\,\$\,\},\;\newline

f(\#/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\#/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\#/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\;\;f(\#/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\;\;\newline
f(\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\$/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\;\;f(\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\#/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\;\;\newline

f(f(\$,\$)/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\$/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}f(\$,\$)/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\;\;\,f(f(\$,\$)/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},f(\$,\$))/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle}\dot{\rightarrow}f(\$,\$)/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle}\;\;\newline
f(\#/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},f(\$,\$)/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\;\;f(\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},f(\$,\$)/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\;\;\newline
f(f(\$,\$)/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\#/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\;\;f(f(\$,\$)/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle}\,\}
.

C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle=\{\,\{\,\#,\;\$,\;\mathsterling,\;\newline
f(\#,\#),\;f(\#,\$),\;f(\#,\mathsterling),\;\newline
f(\$,\#),\;f(\$,\$),\;f(\$,\mathsterling),\;\newline

, and

are total.

Example 5.54
.

We now continue Example
3.21
.

C\langle E,F\rangle=\{\,\#/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle},\;\flat/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}\,\}
,

R\langle E,F\rangle=\{\,\#\dot{\rightarrow}\#/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle},\;\$\dot{\rightarrow}\#/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle},\;\mathsterling\dot{\rightarrow}\#/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle},\;\flat\dot{\rightarrow}\flat/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle},\;\newline
f(\#/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle},\#/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\#/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle},\;f(\#/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle},\flat/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\#/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle},\;f(\flat/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle},\#/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\#\,\}
.

C\langle F,E\rangle=\{\,\#/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\$/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle}\,\}
,

R\langle F,E\rangle=\{\,\#\dot{\rightarrow}\#/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\;\$\dot{\rightarrow}\$/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\;\mathsterling\dot{\rightarrow}\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\;\flat\dot{\rightarrow}\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\;\newline
f(\#/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\#/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\;f(\#/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\$/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\;f(\#/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\;\newline
f(\$/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\#/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\;f(\$/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\$/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\;f(\$/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\;\newline
f(\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\#/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\;f(\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\$/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\;f(\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle}\,\}
.

C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle=\{\,\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\,\}
.

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle=\{\,\#\dot{\rightarrow}\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle},\;\$\dot{\rightarrow}\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle},\;\mathsterling\dot{\rightarrow}\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle},\;\flat\dot{\rightarrow}\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle},\;\newline
f(\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle},\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\,\}
.

We note that
R\langle E,F\rangle
,
R\langle F,E\rangle
, and

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

are total.

.

6
The Auxiliary dpwpa for the GTESs
E
and
F

Recall that
E
and
F
are GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
,
n=\mathrm{size}(E)+\mathrm{size}(F)
, and
W
stands for

ST\langle E\cup F\rangle
.
We define the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])

for the GTESs
E
and
F
.
To simplify our notations, each vertex of the dpwpa
AUX[E;F]
is a constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
i.e, an equivalence class of
\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
Furthermore, we study the
R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
reachability of a constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

from a proper extension of a constant
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.

Definition 6.59
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
.
Then let

INC(E\cup F,E)=\{\,({b},{c})\mid{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,{c}\in C\langle E,F\rangle,\mbox{ and }{c}\subseteq{b}\,\}
,

NUM(E\cup F,E)=\{\,({b},k)\mid{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,k=|\{{c}\in C\langle E,F\rangle\mid{c}\subseteq{b}\,\}|\,\}
,

INC(E\cup F,F)=\{\,({b},{c})\mid{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,{c}\in C\langle F,E\rangle\mbox{ and }{c}\subseteq{b}\,\}
, and

NUM(E\cup F,F)=\{\,({b},k)\mid{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,k=|\{{c}\in C\langle F,E\rangle\mid{c}\subseteq{b}\,\}|\,\}
.

Intuitively,
INC(E\cup F,E)
shows the inclusions between the elements of
C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
and
C\langle E,F\rangle
.

NUM(E\cup F,E)
consists of all pairs
({b},k)
, where the second component
k
is the number of all pairs
in
INC(E\cup F,E)
, with first component
{b}
. Similar statements hold true for
INC(E\cup F,F)
and
NUM(E\cup F,F)
as well.

Statement 6.60
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
. Then

|INC(E\cup F,E)|\leq n
and
|INC(E\cup F,F)|\leq n
.

Proof.

By Statement
4.28
,
|C\langle E,F\rangle|\leq n
. For each
{c}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
, there exists at most one

{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
such that
({b},{c})\in INC(E\cup F,E)
.
Therefore,
|INC(E\cup F,E)|\leq|C\langle E,F\rangle|\leq n
.

We can show similarly that
|INC(E\cup F,F)|\leq|C\langle F,E\rangle|\leq n
.
∎

We get the following statement by direct inspection of Definition
6.59
.

Statement 6.61
.

1. For every
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, there exists a unique ordered pair
({b},{c})\in INC(E\cup F,E)

if and only if
{b}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
. Similarly, for every
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, there exists a unique ordered pair
({b},{c})\in INC(E\cup F,F)

if and only if
{b}\in C\langle F,E\rangle
.

2. For every
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, there exists a unique ordered pair
({b},k)\in NUM(E\cup F,E)
. Here
k=1

if and only if
{b}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
. Similarly, for every
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, there exists a unique ordered pair
({b},\ell)\in NUM(E\cup F,F)
. Here
\ell=1

if and only if
{b}\in C\langle F,E\rangle
.

Definition 6.62
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
, and
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
We say that GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
keeps up with the GTRS

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
writing the constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
if

for every rewrite rule of the form

\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
with

m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\sigma\in\Sigma^{(m)}
, and
{b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,

for every
{c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{m}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
with
{c}_{i}\subseteq{b}_{i}

for every
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,m\,\}
,

there exists a rewrite rule in
R\langle E,F\rangle
of the form

\sigma({c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{c}
for some
{c}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
.

Note that for the constant
{c}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
appearing in the definition above, we have

{c}\subseteq{b}
.
Definition
6.62
also defines the notion that GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
keeps up with the GTRS

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
writing the constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.

Definition 6.63
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
.
The auxiliary dpwpa
for the GTESs
E
and
F
is
AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
, where

A[E;F]=BSTEP\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
We assign the attributes

equal_{E},\;equal_{F},\;keeps_{E},\;keeps_{F},\;visited\_outer\_loop,\;visited\_inner\_loop_{E},\;visited\_inner\_loop_{F},\;and\;visited:\mathrm{Boolean}

to the vertices of
C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
Furthermore, we assign the satellite data

counter_{E},counter_{F}:{\mathbb{N}}

to the rewrite rules of
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.

For each vertex
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, our decision algorithm computes the values of the above attributes such that the following conditions hold:

1.

{b}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}
if and only if

{b}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
.

2.

{b}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}
if and only if

{b}\in C\langle F,E\rangle
.

3.

{b}.keeps_{E}=\mathrm{true}
if and only if
R\langle E,F\rangle
keeps up with
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
writing
{b}
.

4.

{b}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}
if and only if
R\langle F,E\rangle
keeps up with
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
writing
{b}
.

Recall that for each vertex
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
list
Adj({b})
consists of all vertices
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
such that there
exists a rewrite rule
\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}
in
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
with
\sigma\in\Sigma_{m}
,
m\geq 1
, and
{c}={b}_{i}
for some
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,m\,\}
.

Statement 6.64
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
. Then

|A[E;F]|\leq 2n
.

Proof.

By Definition
6.63
, Statement
4.46
, we have

|A[E;F]|=|BSTEP\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle|\leq 2n
.
∎

Intuitively, the basic idea of the proof of the following lemma is similar to that of the pumping lemma for regular languages
[
20
]
,
context-free languages
[
20
]
, and regular tree languages
[
18
]
,
[
21
]
. The main difference from the proof of the pumping lemma is that we iterate contracting the ground term rather than pumping it.
We stop when we get such a small ground term that we cannot contract it any more.
If
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

reaches a constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
from a proper extension of a constant
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
then there exists a
1
-context

\zeta
over
\Sigma
such that
such that

1.

\zeta\neq\diamondsuit
,

2.

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
reaches
{b}
from
\zeta[{c}]
, and

3.

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
reduces the subterms of
\zeta[{c}]
at different prefixes of
\mathrm{addr}(\zeta)
– except possibly for the pair
of
\varepsilon
and
\mathrm{addr}(\zeta)
–
to different constants in
C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.

Thus the length of
\mathrm{addr}(\zeta)
is less than equal to
|C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle|
.

Lemma 6.65
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
.
Let

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
reach a constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
from a proper extension of a constant
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
Then there exists a
1
-context
\zeta
over
\Sigma
, where

(b)

\zeta\neq\diamondsuit
,

(c)

k\geq 1
denotes
\mathrm{length}(\mathrm{addr}(\zeta))
,

(c)

for every
i\in\{\,0,\ldots,k\,\}
,
\alpha_{i}
denotes the prefix of
\mathrm{addr}(\zeta)
of length
i

such that

(d)

\zeta[{c}]{\downarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}={b}
,

(e)

\zeta[{c}]|_{\alpha_{i}}{\downarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\neq\zeta[{c}]|_{\alpha_{j}}{\downarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
for any
0\leq i<j\leq k
such that

1\leq i
or
j\leq k-1
,
and

(f)

k\leq|C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle|
.

Proof.

It is sufficient to prove that there exists a
1
-context
\zeta
over
\Sigma
such that
Conditions (a)–(e) hold. Because for any
1
-context
\zeta
over
\Sigma
satisfying Conditions (a)–(e), Condition (f) holds as well.
By Statement
4.44
,
there exists a
1
-context
\delta
over
\Sigma
such that
\delta\neq\diamondsuit
and
\delta[{c}]{\downarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}={b}
, i.e.
Conditions (a)–(d) hold for
\zeta=\delta
.
We now distinguish two cases:

Case 1:

Conditions (e) holds for
\zeta=\delta
. Then we are done.

Case 2:
Condition (e) does not hold for
\zeta=\delta
.
For every
i\in\{\,0,\ldots,n\,\}
, let
\beta_{i}
denote the prefix of
\mathrm{addr}(\delta)
of length
i
.
There exist
i,j\in\{\,0,\ldots,k\,\}

such that

\left(0\leq i<j\leq k-1\mbox{ or }1\leq i<j\leq k\right)
and

\zeta[{c}]|_{\beta_{i}}{\downarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}=\zeta[{c}]|_{\beta_{j}}{\downarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.

We define
\delta^{\prime}\in CON_{\Sigma,1}
from
\delta
by replacing the subterm
\delta|_{\beta_{i}}
at
\beta_{i}
by the subterm
\delta|_{\beta_{j}}

at
\beta_{j}
. Then

\delta^{\prime}\neq\diamondsuit
,

\mathrm{length}(\mathrm{addr}(\delta^{\prime}))<\mathrm{length}(\mathrm{addr}(\delta))
, and

\delta^{\prime}[{c}]{\downarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}={b}
i.e. Conditions (a)–(d) hold for
\zeta=\delta^{\prime}
.

We now distinguish two cases:

Case 2.1:
Condition (e) holds for
\zeta=\delta^{\prime}
. Then Conditions (a)–(e) hold for
\zeta=\delta^{\prime}
. In this case we are done.

Case 2.2:
Condition (e) does not hold for
\zeta=\delta^{\prime}
. In this case we let
\delta=\delta^{\prime}
and repeat the procedure of contracting
\delta
described above
finitely many times until Condition (e) holds for
\zeta=\delta^{\prime}
. Then Conditions (a)–(e) hold for
\zeta=\delta^{\prime}
.
∎

Intuitively,
we now consider two constants,
{b}
and
{c}
in
C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
and a sequence of rewrite rules in
R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle

of which consecutive applications leads us from
{c}
to

{b}
.
Let
{b},{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
k\geq 1
, and
for each
j=1,\ldots,k
, let

1.

\sigma_{j}({b}_{j1},\ldots,{b}_{jm_{j}})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}_{j}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
,
m_{j}\geq 1
,
be a rewrite rule and

2.

i_{j}\in\{\,1,\ldots,m_{j}\,\}
be an integer

such that

1.

{c}={b}_{1i_{1}}
,

2.

for each
j=1,\ldots,k-1
,
{b}_{j}={b}_{(j+1)i_{j+1}}
, and

3.

{b}={b}_{k}
.

The above sequence of rewrite rules is connected in the sense that we can apply them one after another, the right-hand side of each rule appears on the left-hand side of the next rule.
Furthermore, the consecutive applications of these rewrite rules leads us from the constant
{c}
to the constant

{b}
since
{c}
appears on the left-hand side of the first rule, and
{b}
is the right-hand side of the last rule.
We can apply the above rewrite rules one after another in the following way:

{c}
appears in the left-hand side of the first rewrite rule,

we apply the first rewrite rule to its left-hand side,
\sigma_{1}({b}_{11},\ldots,{b}_{1m_{1}})
, we get the right-hand side
{b}_{1}
,
which appears on the left-hand side of the second rewrite rule,

we apply the second rewrite rule to its left-hand side,
\sigma_{2}({b}_{21},\ldots,{b}_{2m_{2}})
, we get the right-hand side
{b}_{2}
,
which appears on the left-hand side of the third rewrite rule, and son on.

Finally, the right-hand side
{b}_{k-1}
of the last but one rewrite rule appears in the left-hand side of the last rewrite rule,

we apply the last rewrite rule to
its left-hand side,
\sigma_{k}({b}_{k1},\ldots,{b}_{km_{k}})
, we get its right-hand side
{b}_{k}={b}
.

We now state formally and show the following intuitive statement: if
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
reaches a constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
from a proper extension of a constant
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, then
there exists a connected sequence of rewrite rules in
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
that leads us from the constant
{c}
to the constant
{b}
.
Moreover,
{c}
and the
intermediate
constants between
{c}
and
{b}
, appearing as the right-hand sides of the applied rewrite rules except for the last one, are pairwise different.

Lemma 6.66
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
.
Let

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
reach a constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
from a proper extension of a constant
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
Then there exists an integer
1\leq k\leq|C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle|
and
for each
j=1,\ldots,k
, there exists a rewrite rule

\sigma_{j}({b}_{j1},\ldots,{b}_{jm_{j}})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}_{j}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
,
m_{j}\geq 1
, and an integer
i_{j}\in\{\,1,\ldots,m_{j}\,\}

such that

1.

{c}={b}_{1i_{1}}
,

2.

for each
j=1,\ldots,k-1
,
{b}_{j}={b}_{(j+1)i_{j+1}}
,

3.

{c}\not\in\{\,{b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{k-1}\,\}
and
for any
1\leq i<j\leq k-1
,
{b}_{i}\neq{b}_{j}
,
and

4.

{b}={b}_{k}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.65
,
there exists a
1
-context
\zeta
over
\Sigma
, where

(b)

\zeta\neq\diamondsuit
,

(c)

k\geq 1
denotes
\mathrm{length}(\mathrm{addr}(\zeta))
,

(c)

for every
i\in\{\,0,\ldots,k\,\}
,
\alpha_{i}
denotes the prefix of
\mathrm{addr}(\zeta)
of length
i

such that

(d)

\zeta[{c}]{\downarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}={b}
,

(e)

\zeta[{c}]|_{\alpha_{i}}{\downarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\neq\zeta[{c}]|_{\alpha_{j}}{\downarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
for any
0\leq i<j\leq k
such that

1\leq i
or
j\leq k-1
,
and

(f)

k\leq|C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle|
.

As
\mathrm{addr}(\xi)=\alpha_{k}
, we have
\xi[{c}]|_{\alpha_{k}}={c}
.

For each
i\in\{1,\ldots,k-1\,\}
, let

{b}_{i}=\zeta[{c}]|_{\alpha_{i}}{\downarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.

Since

{c}=\zeta[{c}]|_{\alpha_{k}}{\downarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
,
by Condition (e)
we have

{c}\not\in\{\,{b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{k-1}\,\}
and
for any
1\leq i<j\leq k-1
,
{b}_{i}\neq{b}_{j}
.

Then consider a sequence of
R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
reductions, where we indicate the rewrite rules applied at the positions

\alpha_{k-1}
,
\alpha_{k-2}
,
\ldots
,
\alpha_{0}
:

\zeta[{c}]=t_{0}\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}^{*}s_{1}\mathop{\rightarrow}_{[\alpha_{k-1},\sigma_{1}({b}_{11},\ldots,{b}_{1m_{1}})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}_{1}]}t_{1}\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}^{*}s_{2}\mathop{\rightarrow}_{[\alpha_{k-2},\sigma_{2}({b}_{21},\ldots,{b}_{2m_{2}})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}_{2}]}t_{2}\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}^{*}\newline
\cdots\mathop{\rightarrow}_{[\alpha_{1},\sigma_{k-1}({b}_{(k-1)1},\ldots,{b}_{(k-1)m_{1}})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}_{k-1}]}t_{k-1}\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}^{*}s_{k}\mathop{\rightarrow}_{[\alpha_{0},\sigma_{k}({b}_{k1},\ldots,{b}_{km_{k}})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}_{k}]}{b}_{k}={b}
.

Here

1.

s_{i}\in T_{\Sigma\cup C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
for every
i\in\{1,\ldots,k\,\}
, and
t_{i}\in T_{\Sigma\cup C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
for every
i\in\{0,\ldots,k-1\,\}
,

2.

for each
i\in\{1,\ldots,k\,\}
,

{b}_{i}=\zeta[{c}]|_{\alpha_{k-i}}{\downarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
,

3.

{c}={b}_{1i_{1}}
for some
i_{1}\in\{\,1,\ldots,m_{1}\,\}
,

4.

for each
j\in\{1,\ldots,k\,\}
,

\sigma_{j}({b}_{j1},\ldots,{b}_{jm_{j}})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}_{j}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
,
m_{j}\geq 1
,

5.

for each
j=1,\ldots,k-1
, there exists
i_{j+1}\in\{\,1,\ldots,m_{j+1}\,\}
such that

{b}_{j}={b}_{(j+1)i_{j+1}}
, and

6.

{b}={b}_{k}
. ∎

We have the following result by Lemma
6.66
, Statement
4.44
,
and by the definition of
A[E;F]
in Definition
6.63
.

Lemma 6.67
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
.
If
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

reaches a constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
from a proper extension of a constant
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, then there exists
a path of positive length or a cycle from the vertex
{b}
to the vertex
{c}
in the auxiliary dpwpa

AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
.

We now show the converse of Lemma
6.67
.
Intuitively,
if there exists a connected sequence of rewrite rules in
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

that leads us from a constant
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
to a constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
then
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
reaches the constant
{b}
from a proper extension of the constant
{c}
.
By Definition
4.27
, Definition
4.29
, and Proposition
4.33
we have the following lemma.

Lemma 6.68
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
.
Let

\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle

be a rewrite rule, where
m\geq 1
,
{b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m},{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
. Let
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,m\,\}
.
Then there exist ground terms
s_{1},\ldots,s_{i-1},s_{i+1},\ldots,s_{m}\in T_{\Sigma}
such that

\sigma(s_{1},\ldots,s_{i-1},{b}_{i},s_{i+1},\ldots,s_{m})\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}^{*}\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}{b}
.

Lemma 6.69
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
.
Let
k\geq 1
, and

({b}_{k},{b}_{k-1}),\,({b}_{k-1},{b}_{k-2}),\,\ldots,\,({b}_{1},{b}_{0})\in A[E;F]
.
Then there exists a
1
-context
\delta
over
\Sigma
such that
\delta\neq\diamondsuit
and

\delta[{b}_{0}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}^{*}{b}_{k}
.

Proof.

By Definition
6.63
and Definition
4.45
,
for each
j=1,\ldots,k
, there exists a rewrite rule

\sigma_{j}({b}_{j1},\ldots,{b}_{jm_{j}})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}_{j}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
,
m_{j}\geq 1
,
such that

{b}_{0}\in\{\,{b}_{11},\ldots,{b}_{1m_{1}}\,\}
and
for each
j=1,\ldots,k-1
, there exists
i_{j+1}\in\{\,1,\ldots,m_{j+1}\,\}
such that

{b}_{j}={b}_{(j+1)i_{j+1}}
.

We proceed by induction on
k
.

Base Case:

k=1
. By Lemma
6.68
, we are done.

Induction Step:
Let
k\geq 1
, and assume that the statement of the lemma is true for
k
. We now show that it is true for
k+1
.
By the induction hypothesis,
there exists a
1
-context
\delta
over
\Sigma
such that

\delta\neq\diamondsuit
and
\delta[{b}_{0}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}^{*}{b}_{k}
.

By Lemma
6.68
, there exist ground terms
s_{1},\ldots,s_{i-1},s_{i+1},\ldots,s_{m_{k+1}}\in T_{\Sigma}
such that

\sigma_{k+1}(s_{1},\ldots,s_{i_{k+1}-1},{b}_{k},s_{i_{k+1}+1},\ldots,s_{m_{k+1}})=\sigma_{k+1}(s_{1},\ldots,s_{i_{k+1}-1},{b}_{(k+1)i_{k+1}},s_{i_{k+1}+1},\ldots,s_{m_{k+1}})\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}^{*}\newline
\sigma_{k+1}({b}_{(k+1)1},\ldots,{b}_{(k+1)i_{k+1}-1},{b}_{(k+1)i_{k+1}},{b}_{(k+1)i_{k+1}+1},\ldots,{b}_{(k+1)m_{k+1}})\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}{b}_{k+1}
.

Thence we have

\sigma_{k+1}(s_{1},\ldots,s_{i_{k+1}-1},\delta[{b}_{0}],s_{i_{k+1}+1},\ldots,s_{m_{k+1}})\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}^{*}\sigma_{k+1}(s_{1},\ldots,s_{i_{k+1}-1},{b}_{k},s_{i_{k+1}+1},\ldots,s_{m_{k+1}})=\newline
\sigma_{k+1}(s_{1},\ldots,s_{i_{k+1}-1},{b}_{(k+1)i_{k+1}},s_{i_{k+1}+1},\ldots,s_{m_{k+1}})\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}^{*}\newline
\sigma_{k+1}({b}_{(k+1)1},\ldots,{b}_{(k+1)i_{k+1}-1},{b}_{(k+1)i_{k+1}},{b}_{(k+1)i_{k+1}+1},\ldots,{b}_{(k+1)m_{k+1}})\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}{b}_{k+1}
. ∎

We have the following statement
by Lemma
6.69
.

Lemma 6.70
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
.
If there exists
a path of positive length or a cycle from a vertex
{b}
to a vertex
{c}
in the auxiliary dpwpa

AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
, then

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

reaches the constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
from a proper extension of the constant
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.

By Lemma
6.67
and Lemma
6.70
, we have the following result.

Lemma 6.71
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
.

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
reaches a constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
from a proper extension of a constant
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

if and only if there is a path of positive length or a cycle from
the vertex
{b}
to the vertex
{c}

in the auxiliary dpwpa

AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
.

7
Running Examples Further Continued

We continue our running examples, for each one of them we present the dpwpa
AUX[E;F]
.

Example 7.72
.

We now continue Examples
3.18
and
5.51
. We construct
the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
for the GTESs
E
and
F
.
Recall that

C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle=\{\,\{\,\#\,\}
,
\{\,\$\,\}
,

\{\,f(\#),\;g(\#)\,\}
,

\{\,f(\$),\;g(\$)\,\}\,\}
.

Observe that

A[E;F]=\{\,(f(\#)/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle},\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}),\;(f(\$)/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle},\;\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle})\,\}
.

1.

equal_{E},\;equal_{F},\;keeps_{E},\;keeps_{F}
are
attributes of the vertices in
C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.

2.

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}
,

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}
,

f(\#)/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}
, and

f(\$)/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{false}
.

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}
,

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}
,

f(\#)/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{false}
, and

f(\$)/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}
.

3.

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{E}=\mathrm{true}
,

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{E}=\mathrm{true}
,

f(\#)/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}).keeps_{E}=\mathrm{true}
, and

f(\$)/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}).keeps_{E}=\mathrm{true}
.

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}
,

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}
,

f(\#)/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}
, and

f(\$)/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}
.

Example 7.73
.

We now continue Examples
3.19
and
5.52
. We construct
the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
for the GTESs
E
and
F
.
Recall that

C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle=\{\,\{\,\#,\;f(\#),\;f^{2}(\#),\;f^{3}(\#)\,\}\,\}=\{\,W\,\}
.

Observe that

A[E;F]=\{\,(\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle},\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle})\,\}
.

1.

equal_{E},\;equal_{F},\;keeps_{E},\;keeps_{F}
are
attributes of the vertices in
(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
.

2.

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{false}
and

.

3.

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{E}=\mathrm{false}
and

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{false}
.

Example 7.74
.

We now continue Example
3.22
and its sequel
5.55
. We construct
the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
for the GTESs
E
and
F
.
Recall that

C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle=\{\,\{\,\#,\;\$,\;f(\#,\#),\;f(\#,\$),\;f(\$,\#),\;f(\$,\$)\,\}\,\}
.

Observe that

A[E;F])=\{\,(\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle},\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle})\,\}
.

By direct inspection of
C\langle E,F\rangle
,
C\langle F,E\rangle
, and
C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, we get that

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\not\in C\langle E,F\rangle
hence
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{false}
,

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\not\in C\langle F,E\rangle
hence
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{false}
.

By direct inspection of
C\langle E,F\rangle
,
R\langle E,F\rangle
,

C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, and
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, we get that

R\langle E,F\rangle
keeps up with
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
writing

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
, hence

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{E}=\mathrm{true}
.

The rewrite rule

f(\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle},\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}

is in
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
and

f(\#,\$)/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle}\subseteq\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.
However, in
R\langle F,E\rangle

there does not exist a rewrite rule with left-hand side

f(f(\#,\$)/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},f(\#,\$)/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})
. Hence
R\langle F,E\rangle
does not keep up with
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
writing

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
, and

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{false}
.

Example 7.75
.

We now continue Examples
3.23
and
5.56
. We construct
the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
for the GTESs
E
and
F
.
Observe that

A[E;F]=\{\,(\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle},\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}),\;(\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle},\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}),\;(\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle},\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle})\,\}
.

By direct inspection of
C\langle E,F\rangle
,
C\langle F,E\rangle
, and
C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, we get that

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{false}
,

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}
,

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}
,

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{false}
,

We now show that GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
keeps up with GTRS

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
writing
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.
Recall that

1.

\#/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle}=\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
. Furthermore,
\#/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle}
is the only element of
C\langle F,E\rangle
which is a subset of
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.

2.

For the rewrite rule
\#\dot{\rightarrow}\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\in R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, there is a rewrite rule with left-hand side
\#

in
R\langle F,E\rangle
.

3.

For the rewrite rule

f(\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle},\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\in R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, there is a rewrite rule with left-hand side

f(\#/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle},\#/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})
in
R\langle F,E\rangle
.

Consequently,
GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
keeps up with GTRS

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
writing
\#
, and

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}
.

We now show that GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
does not keep up with GTRS

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
writing
\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.
Recall that

1.

g(\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\in R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,

2.

g(g(\$))/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})\subseteq\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle})
, and

3.

in
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, there does not exist a rewrite rule with left-hand side

g(g(g(\$))/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle})
.

Thus
GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
does not keep up with GTRS

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
writing
\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
, and

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{false}
.

8
Constructing the Auxiliary dpwpa for the GTESs
E
and
F

We adopt and slightly modify the fast ground completion algorithm of
Fülöp and Vágvölgyi in Definition
4.29
. This way, we produce the GTRSs
R\langle E,F\rangle
, GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
, and
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, and
construct the sets and relations and dpwpa, which we introduced in Section
6
and our decision procedure needs.

We define a mapping
counter_{E}:R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}
,
to each rewrite rule
\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}
,
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
in
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, we assign the number of rules
\sigma({c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{c}
in
R\langle E,F\rangle
,
where

{c}_{i}\subseteq{b}_{i}
for every
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,m\,\}
.
In pseudocode we treat the nonnegative integer
counter_{E}(\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{b})
as satellite data
of the rewrite rule

\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}
, and denote it as

\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}.counter_{E}
. In this way, we store the mapping
counter_{E}
.
We define the mapping
counter_{F}:R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}
and treat
counter_{F}(\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{b})
in a similar way.

Definition 8.76
.

Algorithm Constructing Auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]

(CAD for short)

Input:
A signature
\Sigma
and GTESs
E
and
F
over
\Sigma
.

,

, and

;

,

, and

stored without duplicates in a red-black tree saved in a variable

,

, the search key is the first component

, the second component,

is satellite data;

stored in a red-black tree saved without duplicates in a variable
RBT2
,
for every ordered pair
({b},{c})\in INC(E\cup F,F)
, the search key is the first component
{b}
, the second component,
{c}
is satellite data;

stored in a red-black tree saved without duplicates in a variable
RBT3
,
for every ordered pair

{b}
, the second component,

is satellite data;

6.

NUM(E\cup F,F)
stored in a red-black tree saved without duplicates in a variable
RBT4

for every ordered pair
({b},k)\in NUM(E\cup F,F)
, the search key is the first component
{b}
, the second component,
k
is satellite data;

the GTRS

stored in a red-black tree saved without duplicates in a variable

;
each node in

, and satellite data

and

stored in a red-black tree saved without duplicates in a variable

;
for every ordered pair
({b},{c})\in BSTEP\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, the search key is the first component
{b}
, the second component,
{c}
is satellite data;

the GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
stored in a red-black tree saved without duplicates in a variable

;

the GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
stored in a red-black tree saved without duplicates in a variable
RBT8
;

the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
for the GTESs
E
and
F
, and
for every vertex

the values of the attributes
{b}.equal_{E}
,
{b}.equal_{F}
,
{b}.keeps_{E}
, and
{b}.keeps_{F}
;

var

i
,
m
: integer;
\sigma
: symbol in
\Sigma
;

{b},{b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{mar_{\Sigma}}:C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
;

{c},{c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{mar_{\Sigma}}:C\langle E,F\rangle
;

{d},{d}_{1},\ldots,{d}_{mar_{\Sigma}}:C\langle F,E\rangle
;

RBT1
,
RBT2
,
RBT3
,

,
RBT6
,
RBT5
,
RBT7
,

: red-black trees.

(Here comes the main part of the algorithm CAD. We run the fast ground completion algorithm
in Definition
4.29
for each of GTESs
E
,
F
, and
E\cup F
, in this way we compute data items 1, 2, 7, 9, and 10 above. During the run of the algorithm CAD, it computes also the other data items listed above. Our algorithm consists of six steps:

1.

We create a subterm dag
G=(V,A,\lambda)
for
E
and
F
.

2.

We compute the relations
INC(E\cup F,E)
,
INC(E\cup F,F)
,

,

,
BSTEP\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

,

, and

.

We compute the relations

and

.

We create the adjacency-list representation of the auxiliary dpwpa

for the GTESs
E
and
F
.
