We compute the values of the attribute
keeps_{E}
.

6.

We compute the values of the attribute
keeps_{F}
.)

⬇

1.{ Create a subterm dag
G=(V,A,\lambda)
for
E
and
F
. }

By Proposition
2.17
, compute the congruence closure
\rho\langle E,F\rangle
of the relation
\tau\langle E,F\rangle=\{\,(u,v)\mid(\hat{u},\hat{v})\in E\,\}.

C\langle E,F\rangle:=\{\,x/_{\rho\langle E,F\rangle}\mid x\in V\,\}
.

By Proposition
2.17
, compute the congruence closure
\rho\langle F,E\rangle
of the relation
\tau\langle F,E\rangle=\{\,(u,v)\mid(\hat{u},\hat{v})\in F\,\}.

C\langle F,E\rangle:=\{\,x/_{\rho\langle F,E\rangle}\mid x\in V\,\}
.

Compute
\tau\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
using the equality
\tau\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle=\tau\langle E,F\rangle\cup\tau\langle F,E\rangle
.

By Proposition
2.17
, compute the congruence closure
\rho\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
of the relation
\tau\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle=\{\,(u,v)\mid(\hat{u},\hat{v})\in E\cup F\,\}.

C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle:=\{\,x/_{\rho\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\mid x\in V\,\}
.

2.{ Computing the relations
INC(E\cup F,E)
,
INC(E\cup F,F)
,
NUM(E\cup F,E)
,
NUM(E\cup F,F)
,
BSTEP\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, the GTRSs
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
R\langle E,F\rangle
,
R\langle F,E\rangle
, the values of the attributes
equal_{E}
,
equal_{F}
for every vertex in
C\langle E,F\rangle
, and the satellite data
counter_{E}
, and
counter_{F}
for every rule in
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
. }

begin

R\langle E,F\rangle:=\emptyset
;

R\langle F,E\rangle:=\emptyset
;

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle:=\emptyset
;

INC(E\cup F,E):=\emptyset
;

INC(E\cup F,F):=\emptyset
;

NUM(E\cup F,E):=\emptyset
;

NUM(E\cup F,F):=\emptyset
;

BSTEP\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle:=\emptyset

end

for
each
{d}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

do

begin

{d}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{\mathrm{false}};

{d}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{\mathrm{false}};

end

for
each
x\in V

do

begin

\sigma:=\lambda(x)
;

m:=outdegree(x);

{b}:=\mathrm{FIND}_{\rho\langle E,F\rangle}(x)
;

{c}:=\mathrm{FIND}_{\rho\langle F,E\rangle}(x)
;

{d}:=\mathrm{FIND}_{\rho\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}(x)
;

INC(E\cup F,E):=INC(E\cup F,E)\cup\{\,({d},{b})\,\}
;

(store
INC(E\cup F,E)
without duplicates in the red-black tree
RBT1
.)

INC(E\cup F,F):=INC(E\cup F,F)\cup\{\,({d},{c})\,\}
;

(store
INC(E\cup F,F)
without duplicates in the red-black tree
RBT2
.)

if

|{b}|=|{d}|

{\bf then}

{d}.equal_{E}:=\mathrm{\mathrm{true}};

if

|{c}|=|{d}|

{\bf then}

{d}.equal_{F}:=\mathrm{\mathrm{true}};

(By Proposition
2.17
we know the cardinalities of all
W/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
-classes,
W/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle}
-classes, and
W/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
-classes.)

for

each

1\leq i\leq m

do

begin

{b}_{i}:=\mathrm{FIND}_{\rho\langle E,F\rangle}(x/i)
;

{c}_{i}:=\mathrm{FIND}_{\rho\langle F,E\rangle}(x/i)
;

{d}_{i}:=\mathrm{FIND}_{\rho\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}(x/i)
;

end

if

\sigma({d}_{1},\ldots,{d}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}\not\in R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

then begin

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle:=R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle\cup\{\,\sigma({d}_{1},\ldots,{d}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}\,\}
;

(Store
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
without duplicates in the red-black tree
RBT5
.)

\sigma({d}_{1},\ldots,{d}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}.counter_{E}:=0
;

\sigma({d}_{1},\ldots,{d}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}.counter_{F}:=0
;

BSTEP\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle:=BSTEP\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle\cup\{\,({d}_{1},{d}),\ldots,({d}_{m},{d})\,\}
;

(Store
BSTEP\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
without duplicates in the red-black tree
RBT6
)

end

if

\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}\not\in R\langle E,F\rangle

then begin

R\langle E,F\rangle:=R\langle E,F\rangle\cup\{\,\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}\,\}
;

(Store
R\langle E,F\rangle
without duplicates in the red-black tree
RBT7
.)

\sigma({d}_{1},\ldots,{d}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}.counter_{E}:=\sigma({d}_{1},\ldots,{d}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}.counter_{E}+1
;

end

if

\sigma({c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{c}\not\in R\langle F,E\rangle

then begin

R\langle F,E\rangle:=R\langle F,E\rangle\cup\{\,\sigma({c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{c}\,\}
;

(Store
R\langle F,E\rangle
without duplicates in the red-black tree
RBT8
.)

\sigma({d}_{1},\ldots,{d}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}.counter_{F}:=\sigma({d}_{1},\ldots,{d}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}.counter_{F}+1
;

end

end

3. { Computing the relations
NUM(E\cup F,E)
and
NUM(E\cup F,F)
. }

Read the nodes of the red-black tree
RBT1
in search key order, during this process create the red-black tree
RBT3
containing without duplicates the relation
NUM(E\cup F,E)
;

Read the nodes of the red-black tree
RBT2
in search key order, during this process create the red-black tree
RBT4
containing without duplicates the relation
NUM(E\cup F,F)
;

4. { Creating the adjacency-list representation of the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
for the GTESs
E
and
F
}

We build the adjacency-list representation of the dpwpa
AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
by reading in search key order the nodes of the black-red tree
RBT6
storing the relation
BSTEP\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
. Recall that for every ordered pair
({b},{c})\in BSTEP\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, the search key is the first component
{b}
, the second component,
{c}
is satellite data.

5. { Computing the values of the attribute
keeps_{E}
. }

for

each

{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

do

{b}.keeps_{E}:=\mathrm{true}
;

for

each
rewrite rule
\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}\in R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
with
m\in{\mathbb{N}}

do

if

\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}.counter_{E}\neq\Pi_{i=1}^{m}|{b}_{i}/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}|

then

{b}.keeps_{E}:=\mathrm{false}

6. { Computing the values of the attribute
keeps_{F}
. }

for

each

{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

do

{b}.keeps_{F}:=\mathrm{true}
;

for

each
rewrite rule
\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}\in R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
with
m\in{\mathbb{N}}

do

if

\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}.counter_{F}\neq\Pi_{i=1}^{m}|{b}_{i}/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle}|

then

{b}.keeps_{F}:=\mathrm{false}

Lemma 8.77
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
.

1. Algorithm CAD on
\Sigma
,
E
, and
F
produces

1.

the relations
\rho\langle E,F\rangle
,
\rho\langle F,E\rangle
, and
\rho\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
;

2.

the set of nullary symbols
C\langle E,F\rangle
,
C\langle F,E\rangle
, and

C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
;

3.

INC(E\cup F,E)
stored without duplicate values in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT1
;

4.

INC(E\cup F,F)
stored without duplicate values in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT2
;

5.

NUM(E\cup F,E)
stored without duplicate values in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT3
;

6.

NUM(E\cup F,F)
stored without duplicate values in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT4
;

7.

the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
stored without duplicate values in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT5
,
each node in
RBT5
contains a search key which is a rule in
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, and satellite data
counter_{E}
and
counter_{F}
;

8.

BSTEP\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
stored without duplicate values in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT6
;
for every ordered pair
({b},{c})\in BSTEP\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, the search key is the first component
{b}
, the second component,
{c}
is satellite data;

9.

the GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
stored without duplicate values in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT7
;

10.

the GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
stored without duplicate values in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT8
;

11.

the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
for the GTESs
E
and
F
.

2. Algorithm CAD on
\Sigma
,
E
, and
F
decides
for every constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
whether
{b}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
and whether
{b}\in C\langle F,E\rangle
, and
sets the value of the attribute
{b}.equal_{E}
to true if and only if
{b}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
, and sets the value of the attribute
{b}.equal_{F}
to true if and only if
{b}\in C\langle F,E\rangle
.

3. Algorithm CAD on
\Sigma
,
E
, and
F
decides for any
{d}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
and any rewrite rule

\sigma({d}_{1},\ldots,{d}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
with right-hand side
{d}
,
whether for all
{b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
such that

{b}_{i}\subseteq{d}_{i}
for every
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,m\,\}
,
there exists a rewrite rule in
R\langle E,F\rangle
with left-hand side

\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})
. If so, then it sets the value of the attribute
{d}.keeps_{E}
to
\mathrm{true}
, otherwise it sets
the value of the attribute
{d}.keeps_{E}
to
\mathrm{false}
.

4. Algorithm CAD on
\Sigma
,
E
, and
F
decides for any
{d}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
and any rewrite rule

\sigma({d}_{1},\ldots,{d}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
with right-hand side
{d}
,
whether for all
{c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{m}\in C\langle F,E\rangle
such that

{c}_{i}\subseteq{d}_{i}
for every
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,m\,\}
,
there exists a rewrite rule in
R\langle F,E\rangle
with left-hand side

\sigma({c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{m})
. If so, then it sets the value of the attribute
{d}.keeps_{F}
to
\mathrm{true}
, otherwise it sets
the value of the attribute
{d}.keeps_{F}
to
\mathrm{false}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.48
and by direct inspection of Definition
8.76
, we obtain Statement 1.

By Proposition
2.17
, we know the cardinality of all
W/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
-classes,
W/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle}
-classes, and

W/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
-classes. For all
{b}\in R\langle E,F\rangle

and

{d}\in R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
such that
{b}\cap{d}\neq\emptyset
, we have
{b}={d}
if and only if
|{b}|=|{d}|
.
Hence we have Statement 2.

We now show Statement 3.
For every rewrite rule
\sigma({d}_{1},\ldots,{d}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}\in R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, we decide
whether for all
{b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
with
{b}_{1}\subseteq{d}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m}\subseteq{d}_{m}
,
there exists a rewrite rule with left-hand side

\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})
in
R\langle E,F\rangle
. When constructing the
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
, whenever we add to
R\langle E,F\rangle
a new rule with left-hand side
\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})
,
where
{b}_{1}\subseteq{b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m}\subseteq{d}_{m}
,
we add
1
to the
attribute
\sigma({d}_{1},\ldots,{d}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}.counter_{E}
.
In this way, we count the rules in
R\langle E,F\rangle
with left-hand side

\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})
,
where
{b}_{1}\subseteq{d}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m}\subseteq{d}_{m}
,
and store the result in the attribute
\sigma({d}_{1},\ldots,{d}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}.counter_{E}
.
Then
\sigma({d}_{1},\ldots,{d}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}.counter_{E}=\Pi_{i=1}^{m}|{d}_{i}/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}|
if and only if
for all
{b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m}\in C\langle E,F\rangle

with
{b}_{1}\subseteq{d}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m}\subseteq{d}_{m}
,
there exists a rewrite rule with left-hand side
\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})

in
R\langle E,F\rangle
.
When Algorithm CAD terminates, for every vertex
{d}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,

{d}.keeps_{E}=\mathrm{true}
if and only if

for every rewrite rule of the form

\sigma({d}_{1},\ldots,{d}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
,

for every
{b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
with
{b}_{i}\subseteq{d}_{i}

for all
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,m\,\}
,

there exists a rewrite rule in
R\langle E,F\rangle
of the form

\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}
for some
{b}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
.

The proof of Satement 4 is similar to that of Statement 3.
∎

Statement 8.78
.

1. We can decide in
O(n)
time whether
for all
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
{b}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
.

2. We can decide in
O(n)
time whether
for all
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
{b}\in C\langle F,E\rangle
.

Proof.

First we show Statement 1. We read each nodes of the red-black tree
RBT3
in lexicographic order.
For every
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, there exists a unique ordered pair
({b},k)\in NUM(E\cup F,E)
. If
k=1
,
then
by Statement
6.61
,
{b}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
. Otherwise,
{b}\not\in C\langle E,F\rangle
.

We can show Statement 2 similarly to Statement 1.
∎

Lemma 8.79
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
.
Algorithm CAD runs in
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
time on
\Sigma
,
E
, and
F
,
where
n=\mathrm{size}(E)+\mathrm{size}(F)
.

Proof.

We show Statements
8.80
–
8.88
.

Statement 8.80
.

The time complexity of constructing the red-black trees
RBT1
and
RBT2
is
O(n)
.

Proof.

By Statement
6.60
,
INC(E\cup F,E)\leq n
. Consequently,
when we construct the red-black tree
RBT1
, we carry out at most
n
insertion. By Proposition
2.2
,
we construct the red-black tree
RBT1
in
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
time.
Similarly, we construct the red-black tree
RBT2
in
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
time.
∎

By Statement
2.5
, we have the following result.

Statement 8.81
.

The time complexity of constructing the relation
\tau\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

and the GTES
E\cup F
stored without duplicate values in a red-black tree saved in the variable
RBT5
,
is
O(n^{2})
time.

We carry out
2n
comparisons of nonnegative numbers in
O(n)
time. Hence we have the following fact.

Statement 8.82
.

The time complexity of computing the value attributes
{b}.equal_{E}
and
{b}.equal_{F}
for all constants
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, is
O(n)
.

By Statement 2 and Statement 3 of
4.48
, we have the following fact.

Statement 8.83
.

The time complexity of constructing the following data is
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
.

1.

the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
stored without duplicate values in a red-black tree saved in the variable
RBT5
,
each node in
RBT5
contains a search key which is a rule in
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
;

2.

BSTEP\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
stored in a red-black tree saved in the variable
RBT6
;

3.

the GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
stored without duplicate values in a red-black tree saved in the variable
RBT7
;

4.

the GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
stored without duplicate values in a red-black tree saved in the variable
RBT8
;

Statement 8.84
.

The time complexity of constructing

1.

NUM(E\cup F,E)
stored without duplicate values in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT3
, and

2.

NUM(E\cup F,F)
stored without duplicate values in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT4

is
O(n)
.

Proof.

When we construct the red-black tree
RBT3
, we read the whole red-black tree
RBT1
in search key order in
O(n)
time. Each modification takes
constant time.
By Statement
6.60
,
|INC(E\cup F,E)|\leq n
. Consequently, we construct the red-black tree
RBT3
in
O(n)
time.
Similarly, we construct the red-black tree
RBT4
in
O(n)
time.
∎

Statement 8.85
.

The time complexitity of computing the values of
the satellite data
counter_{E}
, and
counter_{F}
for all rules in
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

is
O(n)
.

Proof.

When Algorithm CAD computes the values of the satellite data

counter_{E}
and
counter_{F}
for all rules of the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
it carries out at most
2n
additions, since

|C\langle E,F\rangle|\leq|W|\leq n
and
|C\langle F,E\rangle|\leq|W|\leq n
.
∎

Statement 8.86
.

The time complexity of constructing the
auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])

is
O(n)
.

Proof.

We build the adjacency-list representation of the dpwpa
AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
by reading in search key order the nodes of the black-red tree
RBT6

storing the relation
BSTEP\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
. Recall that for every ordered pair

({b},{c})\in BSTEP\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, the search key is the first component
{b}
, the second component,
{c}
is satellite
data.
Then the time complexity of building the adjacency-list representation of the dpwpa
AUX[E;F]
is

O(|C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle|+|A[E;F]|)
.
By Statement
4.28
,

|C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle|\leq|W|\leq n
.
By Statement
6.64
,

|A[E;F]|=|BSTEP\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle|\leq\mathrm{size}(E)+size(F)=2n
.
Therefore the time complexity of building the adjacency-list representation of the dpwpa
AUX[E;F]

is
O(n)
.
∎

Statement
8.78
implies the following fact.

Statement 8.87
.

The time complexitity of computing the values of the attributes
equal_{E}

and
equal_{F}
of all constants
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
is
O(n)
.

Statement 8.88
.

The time complexitity of computing the values of the attributes
keeps_{E}
and

keeps_{F}
of all constants
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
is
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
time.

Proof.

We now estimate the number of operations when computing
the values of the attributes
{b}.keeps_{E}
for all
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
For
each rewrite rule

\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}\in R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
Algorithm CAD carries out
m
searches in the red-black tree
RBT3

and less than or equal to
m
multiplications.
By Statement
4.46
,

\mathrm{size}(R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle)\leq 2n
.
In this way, when we compute the values of the attribute
keeps_{E}
for all rewrite rules

\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}\in R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, the number of searches in the red-black tree
RBT3

is less than equal to
n
. Consequently, by Proposition
2.1
,
the searches in the red-black tree
RBT3
take
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
time.

Furthermore,
the number of multiplications is less than or equal to

\sum_{\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}\in R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}m\leq\mathrm{size}(R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle)\leq 2n
by Statement
4.46
.
Thus, the number of multiplications is less than or equal to
2n
.

For each
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, the number of comparisons of nonnegative integers during computing the value of the attribute
{b}.keeps_{E}
is equal to
the number of rules in
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
with right-hand side
{b}
.
Consequently, the number of comparisons of nonnegative integers during computing the values of the attributes
{b}.keeps_{E}
for all
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
is equal to
|R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle|
. By Statement
4.46

|R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle|\leq n
.
Consequently the time complexity of computing for all
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
the values of the attribute
{b}.keeps_{E}
is
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
.
Similarly, the time complexity of computing for all
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, the values of the attribute
{b}.keeps_{F}
is

O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
.
∎

The Lemma follows from Statements
8.80
–
8.88
.
∎

9
Preparatory Results

The decidability and complexity results of the following sections require some preparatory results which we group together here.

Proposition 9.89
.

Let
A
be a set,
{b}\in A
, and let
\rho,\tau,\omega
be
equivalence relations on
A
such that
{b}/_{\rho}\subset{b}/_{\omega}
and

{b}/_{\tau}\subset{b}/_{\omega}
. Then
{b}/_{\rho}\cup{b}/_{\tau}\subset{b}/_{\omega}
.

Proof.

There exist
{c},{d}\in A
, such that
{c}\in{b}/_{\omega}\setminus{b}/_{\rho}
and

{d}\in{b}/_{\omega}\setminus{b}/_{\tau}
.
We now distinguish three cases.

Case 1:

({c},{d})\in\rho
. Then by the definition of
{c}
,
({b},{d})\not\in\rho
. By the definition of
{d}
,
({b},{d})\not\in\tau
. Thence

({b},{d})\not\in\rho\cup\tau
.

Case 2:

({c},{d})\in\tau
. Then by the definition of
{d}
,

({b},{c})\not\in\tau
. By the definition of
{c}
,
({b},{c})\not\in\rho
. Consequently

({b},{c})\not\in\rho\cup\tau
.

Case 3:

({c},{d})\not\in\rho\cup\tau
. Then by the definition of
{c}
and
{d}
,

({c},{d})\in\omega
, and hence

({c},{d})\in\omega\setminus(\rho\cup\tau)
.

In all three cases we have
{b}/_{\rho}\cup{b}/_{\tau}\subset{b}/_{\omega}
.
∎

Proposition 9.90
.

Let
A
be a set,
{b}\in A
, and let
\rho,\tau,\omega
be
equivalence relations on
A
such that

{b}/_{\rho}\cup{b}/_{\tau}={b}/_{\omega}
. Then

{b}/_{\rho}={b}/_{\omega}
or
{b}/_{\tau}={b}/_{\omega}
.

Proof.

By contradiction, assume that
{b}/_{\rho}\neq{b}/_{\omega}
and
{b}/_{\tau}\neq{b}/_{\omega}
.
Then
{b}/_{\rho}\subset{b}/_{\omega}
and

{b}/_{\tau}\subset{b}/_{\omega}
. Consequently, by Proposition
9.89
,

{b}/_{\rho}\cup{b}/_{\tau}\subset{b}/_{\omega}
.
∎

Lemma 9.91
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
.
If
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
is total, then
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

is total as well.

Proof.

Let
\sigma\in\Sigma_{m}
,
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
, and
{b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
. Then there exist
t_{1},\ldots,t_{m}\in W
such that
for every
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,m\,\}
,
t_{i}/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}={b}_{i}
.
As GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
is total, there exists a rewrite rule

\sigma(t_{1}/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle},\ldots,t_{m}/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}t/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
in
R\langle E,F\rangle
. Accordingly, by the definition of
R\langle E,F\rangle
,

\sigma(t_{1},\ldots,t_{m})\in W
and

\sigma(t_{1},\ldots,t_{m})\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}t
. Then
\sigma(t_{1},\ldots,t_{m})\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}t
.
For every
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,m\,\}
,

t_{i}/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
Thus, by the definition of
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, the rewrite rule

\sigma(t_{1}/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle},\ldots,t_{m}/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}t/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
is in
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
∎

Proposition 9.92
.

For any
GTESs
E
and
F
over a signature
\Sigma
,
the following four statements are equivalent:

1.

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

2.

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}=\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

3.

There exists a GTES
H
over
\Sigma
such that
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{H}=\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

4.

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
is a congruence on the ground term algebra
{\cal T}(\Sigma)
.

Proof.

(1\Rightarrow 2)
For the GTES
E\cup F
, we have

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}
.
Thence
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}=\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

(2\Rightarrow 3)
For the GTES
E\cup F
, we have
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}=\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

(3\Rightarrow 4)

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{H}
is a congruence on the ground term algebra
{\cal T}(\Sigma)
, hence
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
is a congruence on the ground term algebra
{\cal T}(\Sigma)
.

(4\Rightarrow 1)

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
is a congruence on the ground term algebra
{\cal T}(\Sigma)
.
Since
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}
is the least congruence on
T_{\Sigma}
containing
E\cup F
,
we have

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.
∎

In light of Proposition
9.92
, from now on we decide for
GTESs
E
and
F
over a signature
\Sigma
, whether
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

Lemma 9.93
.

Let
E
and
F
be GTESs over a unary signature
\Sigma
. Let
W=ST\langle E\cup F\rangle
.

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}

if and only if
for each
p\in W
,
\left(p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}\mbox{ or }p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}\right)
.

Proof.

(
\Rightarrow
) If
W=\emptyset
, then we are done. Assume that
W\neq\emptyset
.
Let
p\in W
,
s\in T_{\Sigma}
, and
p\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}s
. Then

p\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}s
or
p\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}s
. Consequently

p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}\subseteq p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}\cup p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}
.
Since
p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}\cup p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}\subseteq p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}
, we have
p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}\cup p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}
.
By Proposition
9.90
,
p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
or

p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}
.

(
\Leftarrow
)
Let
s,t\in T_{\Sigma}
be arbitrary such that
s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}t
. Then

s=q_{0}\mathop{\leftrightarrow}_{[\alpha_{1},(l_{1},r_{1})]}q_{1}\mathop{\leftrightarrow}_{[\alpha_{2},(l_{2},r_{2})]}q_{2}\mathop{\leftrightarrow}_{[\alpha_{3},(l_{3},r_{3})]}\cdots\mathop{\leftrightarrow}_{[\alpha_{n},(l_{n},r_{n})]}q_{n}=t

for some
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

where for every
i\in\{\,0,\ldots,n\,\}
,
q_{i}\in T_{\Sigma}
and for every
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,n\,\}
,

l_{i}\doteq r_{i}\in E\cup F
.
We now show that

s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}t
or

s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}t
.

If
n=0
, then
s=t
and hence
s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}t
. Let us assume that
n\geq 1
.
Let
\alpha_{i}
be a shortest word in the set

\{\,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\ldots,\alpha_{n}\,\}
, where
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,n\,\}
. Then
there exist

\delta\in CON_{\Sigma,1}
,
v,z\in T_{\Sigma}
such that

1.

addr(\delta)=\alpha_{i}
,

2.

s=\delta[v]
,

t=\delta[z]
, and

3.

q_{i-1}=\delta[l_{i}]
,
q_{i}=\delta[r_{i}]
.

So,

v\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}l_{i}\mathop{\leftrightarrow}_{E\cup F}r_{i}\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}z
or
v\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}r_{i}\mathop{\leftrightarrow}_{E\cup F}l_{i}\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}z
.

Consequently,

v,z\in l_{i}/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}
.
As

l_{i}/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=l_{i}/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
or

l_{i}/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=l_{i}/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}
, we have

v,z\in l_{i}/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
or
v,z\in l_{i}/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}
.
Therefore

v\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}z
or

v\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}z
.

Then

s=\delta[v]\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\delta[z]=t
or

s=\delta[v]\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}\delta[z]=t
. ∎

By Proposition
2.11
and Proposition
2.12
we have the following result.

Proposition 9.94
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
, and
n=\mathrm{size}(E)+\mathrm{size}(F)
.
Let
W=ST\langle E\cup F\rangle
.
We can decide in
O(n^{3})
time whether for each
p\in W
,

p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
or

p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}
.

Lemma 9.95
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
such that GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
is total. Let
W=ST\langle E\cup F\rangle
.
Then
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}

if and only if for each
p\in W
,

p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
or

p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}
.

Proof.

(
\Rightarrow
)
Let
p\in W
,
s\in T_{\Sigma}
, and
p\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}s
. Then

p\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}s
or
p\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}s
. Consequently

p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}\subseteq p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}\cup p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}
.
Observe that

p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}\cup p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}\subseteq p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}
.
Thus
p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}\cup p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}
.
By Proposition
9.90
,
p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
or

p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}
.

(
\Leftarrow
)
Let
s,t\in T_{\Sigma}
be arbitrary such that
s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}t
. Then by Lemma
4.42
,

s\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}^{*}p/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
for some
p\in W
. Consequently,

s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}p
.
Since
s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}t
,
we have
t\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}p
.
Then by our assumption,

s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}p
and
t\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}p
or

s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}p
and
t\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}p
.

For this reason
s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}t
or
s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}t
. Thence
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.
∎

Lemma 9.96
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
such that GTRS

R\langle E,F\rangle

is total. Let
W=ST\langle E\cup F\rangle
. Then for each
p\in W
,

p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}\mbox{ if and only if }p/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}=p/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
.

Proof.

(
\Rightarrow
) Let
p\in W
. By the definition of
{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
and
{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
,

p/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}=p_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}\cap T_{\Sigma}\times T_{\Sigma}
and

p/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}=p_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}\cap T_{\Sigma}\times T_{\Sigma}
.

Thus,

p/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}=p/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
.

(
\Leftarrow
)
Let
p\in W
.
Since
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}
, we have
p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}\subseteq p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}
. Our aim is to show that

p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}\subseteq p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
. To this end, let

s\in T_{\Sigma}
be such that
s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}p
. We now show that
s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}p
as well.
As
R\langle E,F\rangle
is total, by Lemma
4.42
,

s{\downarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}=t/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
for some
t\in W
.

By Propositon
4.35
,
s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}t
.
Then

t\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}p
.

Thus

t\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}p
.

Since
t,p\in W
, by the definition of
\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, we have

(t,p)\in{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.

As
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}
, we have

t_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}\subseteq t_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}=p_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}=p/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
.

Consequently

t/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}\subseteq p_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
.

Thence

t/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}=p_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
.

Consequently,
s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}t\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}p
.
For this reason, by the definition of
s
,

p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}\subseteq p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
.

Thus,

p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
.
∎

Lemma 9.97
.

Let
E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
such that
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
are total.
Then

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}

if and only if for each
p\in W
,

\left(p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}\mbox{ or }p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}\right)
.

Proof.

By Lemma
9.91
,

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

is total as well. Consequently, by Lemma
9.95
and Lemma
9.96
, we have the lemma.
∎

10
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

We now introduce the notion of GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle

simulating the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
on a constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
We study the case when GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
is total.
Furthermore, we introduce the
concept of GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
Then we describe the connection between the latter concept and
the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
.

Definition 10.98
.

Let
E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
, and let
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
We say that GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle

simulates the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
on the constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
if Conditions 1 and 2 hold:

1.

{b}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
.

2.

For every
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
if

R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
reaches
{b}
from a proper extension of the constant
{c}
, then

for every rewrite rule of the form

\sigma({c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{c}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
with
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

for all
{d}_{1},\ldots,{d}_{m}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
with

{d}_{i}\subseteq{c}_{i}
for all
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,m\,\}
,

there exists
{d}\in C\langle E,F\rangle

such that the rewrite rule

\sigma({d}_{1},\ldots,{d}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}
is in
R\langle E,F\rangle
.

Statement 10.99
.

{d}\subseteq{c}
for
{d},{c}
appearing in Condition 2 in Definition
10.98
.

Intuitively, in Condition 2 in Definition
10.98
, the rewrite rule

\sigma({d}_{1},\ldots,{d}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}
in
R\langle E,F\rangle
matches the rewrite rule

\sigma({c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{c}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
.

By direct inspection of Definition
4.30
and
Definition
10.98
, we get the following statement.

Statement 10.100
.

Let
E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
such that
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
is total, and let
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle

simulates the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
on the constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
if and only if
{b}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
.

Definition 10.101
.

Let
E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
.
We say that GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

if
for each constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
or GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
on
{b}
.

We have the following statement by direct inspection of Definition
10.101
and Statement
10.100
.

Statement 10.102
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
such that GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
is total.
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

if and only if for each constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
Conditions 1 or 2 hold:

1.
{b}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
.

2.
{b}\in C\langle F,E\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

simulates the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
on
{b}
.

By Definitions
10.98
and
10.101
, and Lemma
6.71
, and Definition
6.62
, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 10.103
.

Let
E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
.
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

if and only if for each constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
Conditions 1 or 2 hold:

1.
{b}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
and
for each
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
if there is a path of positive length or a cycle from

{b}
to
{c}

in the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
, then

R\langle E,F\rangle
keeps up with
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
writing
{c}
.

2.
{b}\in C\langle F,E\rangle
and
for each
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
if there is a path of positive length or a cycle from

{b}
to
{c}

in the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
, then

R\langle F,E\rangle
keeps up with
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
writing
{c}
.

By Statement
10.102
, Definition
10.98
, Lemma
6.71
, and Definition
6.62

we have the following lemma.

Lemma 10.104
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
such that
R\langle E,F\rangle
is total.
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

if and only if for each constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
Conditions 1 or 2 hold:

1.
{b}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
.

2.
{b}\in C\langle F,E\rangle
and
for each constant
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
if there is a path of positive length or a cycle from

{b}
to
{c}

in the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
, then

R\langle F,E\rangle
keeps up with
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
writing
{c}
.

Example 10.105
.

We now continue Example
3.18
, Example
5.51
, and Example
7.72
.
There exist two paths of positive length in
AUX[E;F]
.
There do not exist cycles in
AUX[E;F]
.

The length of the first path is
1
, and it starts from the vertex

f(\#)/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
, and leads to
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.
By Lemma
6.71
,

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
reaches
f(\#)/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}

from a proper extension of
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.
Recall that

f(\#)/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}
and

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{E}=\mathrm{true}
.

The length of the second path is
1
, and it starts from the vertex
f(\$)/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
,
and leads to
\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.
By Lemma
6.71
,

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
reaches
f(\$)/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
from a proper extension of

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
. Recall that

f(\$)/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}
and

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}
.

Observe that Conditions 1 or 2 in Lemma
10.103

hold true for each vertex in

C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle=\{\,\{\,\#\,\}
,
\{\,\$\,\}
,

\{\,f(\#),\;g(\#)\,\}
,

\{\,f(\$),\;g(\$)\,\}\,\}
. Thence by Lemma
10.103
, GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.

Example 10.106
.

We now continue Example
3.19
, Example
5.52
, and Example
7.73
.
There does not exists a path of positive length in
AUX[E;F]
.
There exists one cycle in
AUX[E;F]
.
It is of length
1
, and it starts from the vertex
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
, and leads to

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.
By Lemma
6.71
,

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
reaches

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
from a proper extension of
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.
Recall that

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{false}
,

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{false}
, and

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{E}=\mathrm{false}
,

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{false}
.

Observe that neither Condition 1 in nor Condition 2 in Lemma
10.103
holds
for the vertex
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.
Thus by Lemma
10.103
, GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together do not simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.

Intuitively, assume that GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
simulates the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

on a vertex
{d}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
of
AUX[E;F]
.
Consider a term
\delta[{b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{k}]

for some
\delta\in CON_{\Sigma,k}
,
{b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{k}\in C\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
and a sequence of reductions

(i)

\delta[{b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{k}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}^{*}{d}
.

Furthermore, consider any constants
{c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{k}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
such that

{c}_{j}\subseteq{b}_{j}
for every
j\in\{\,1,\ldots,k\,\}
. We substitute
{c}_{j}
for
{b}_{j}
in
\delta[{b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{k}]
, in this way, we get the term

\delta[{c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{k}]
. Since
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
simulates the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
we construct a fitting sequence of reductions

(ii)

\delta[{c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{k}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}^{*}{e}

for some
{e}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
. We obtain (ii) following step by step the reductions in (i). For each reduction of (i),
we apply a fitting rule at the same position in (ii). Then for each reduction step,
for the two terms
s
and
t
obtained in (i) and (ii), respectively,
each constant
{c}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
appearing in
t
corresponds to the constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

appearing at the same position in
s
.
Moreover
{c}
, as congruence class, is
subset of the corresponding constant
{b}
. Finally, constant
{e}
corresponds to constant
{d}
, and
{e}\subseteq{d}
.
Formally, we state and prove the following result.

Lemma 10.107
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
such that GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
simulates the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

on a constant
{d}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
Let Conditions (a) and (b) hold.

(a)

\delta_{n}[{b}_{n1},\ldots,{b}_{nk_{n}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{n-1}[{b}_{(n-1)1},\ldots,{b}_{(n-1)k_{n-1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{n-2}[{b}_{(n-2)1},\ldots,{b}_{(n-2)k_{n-2}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\cdots\newline
\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{1}[{b}_{11},\ldots,{b}_{1k_{1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}{d}
,

where

n\geq 1
,
for every
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,n\,\}
,

\delta_{i}\in CON_{\Sigma,{k_{i}}}
,
k_{i}\in{\mathbb{N}}
, and
{b}_{i1},\ldots,{b}_{i{k_{i}}}\in C\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
.

(b)

For every
j\in\{\,1,\ldots,k_{n}\,\}
,
{c}_{nj}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
and
{c}_{nj}\subseteq{b}_{nj}
.

Then
for any
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,n-1\,\}
,
j\in\{\,1,\ldots,k_{i}\,\}
, there exist
{c}_{ij}\in C\langle E,F\rangle

with
{c}_{ij}\subseteq{b}_{ij}
, and there exists
{e}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
with
{e}\subseteq{d}

such that the following sequence of reductions holds:

\delta_{n}[{c}_{n_{1}},\ldots,{c}_{nk_{n}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}\delta_{n-1}[{c}_{(n-1)1},\ldots,{c}_{(n-1)k_{n-1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}\delta_{n-2}[{c}_{(n-2)1},\ldots,{c}_{(n-2)k_{n-2}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}\cdots\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}\newline
\delta_{1}[{c}_{11},\ldots,{c}_{1k_{1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}{e}
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
n
.

Base Case:

n=1
. Assume that Conditions (a) and (b) hold. Then
\delta_{1}=\sigma(\diamondsuit,\ldots,\diamondsuit)
for some
\sigma\in\Sigma_{k_{1}}
,

k_{1}\in{\mathbb{N}}
, where

\delta_{1}[{b}_{11},\ldots,{b}_{1k_{1}}]=\sigma({b}_{11},\ldots,{b}_{1k_{1}})
,

\sigma({b}_{11},\ldots,{b}_{1k_{1}})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
,   and

\sigma({b}_{11},\ldots,{b}_{1k_{1}})\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}{d}
.

By Condition (b), for every
j\in\{\,1,\ldots,k_{1}\,\}
,
{c}_{1j}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
and
{c}_{1j}\subseteq{b}_{1j}
.
We assumed that
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
simulates the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

on the constant
{d}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
Consequently by the rewrite rule
\sigma({b}_{11},\ldots,{b}_{1k_{1}})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
and Definition
10.98
, and Statement
10.99
, there exists a rewrite rule

\sigma({c}_{11},\ldots,{c}_{1k_{1}})\dot{\rightarrow}{c}\in R\langle E,F\rangle
, where
{c}\subseteq{d}
.

Since

\delta_{1}[{c}_{11},\ldots,{c}_{1k_{1}}]=\sigma({c}_{11},\ldots,{c}_{1k_{1}})
, we have

\delta_{1}[{c}_{11},\ldots,{c}_{1k_{1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}{c}
,
where
{c}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
and
{c}\subseteq{d}
.

Induction Step:
Let
n\geq 1
, and assume that the statement of the claim is true for
n
. We now show
that it is also true for
n+1
as well.
Assume that Conditions (
\mathrm{a}^{\prime}
) and (
\mathrm{b}^{\prime}
) hold:

(
\mathrm{a}^{\prime}
)

\delta_{n+1}[{b}_{(n+1)1},\ldots,{b}_{(n+1)k_{n+1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{n}[{b}_{n1},\ldots,{b}_{nk_{n}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{n-1}[{b}_{(n-1)1},\ldots,{b}_{(n-1)k_{n-1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\newline
\cdots\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{2}[{b}_{21},\ldots,{b}_{2k_{2}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{1}[{b}_{11},\ldots,{b}_{1k_{1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}{d}
,

where
for every
i\in\{\,0,\ldots,n+1\,\}
,

\delta_{i}\in CON_{\Sigma,{k_{i}}}
,
k_{i}\in{\mathbb{N}}
, and
{b}_{i1},\ldots,{b}_{i{k_{i}}}\in C\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
.

(
\mathrm{b}^{\prime}
)

For every
j\in\{\,1,\ldots,k_{n+1}\,\}
,
{c}_{(n+1)j}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
and
{c}_{(n+1)j}\subseteq{b}_{(n+1)j}
.

Then Conditions (c)–(f) hold:

(c)

We obtain
\delta_{n+1}
from
\delta_{n}
by replacing the
j
th occurrence from left to right of the hole

\diamondsuit
by the context

\sigma(\diamondsuit,\ldots,\diamondsuit)
, where

j\in\{\,1,\ldots,k_{n}\,\}
,
\sigma\in\Sigma_{m}
,
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
, and
k_{n}=k_{n+1}-m+1
.

(d)

\delta_{n+1}[{b}_{(n+1)1},\ldots,{b}_{(n+1)k_{n+1}}]=\delta_{n}[{b}_{(n+1)1},\ldots,{b}_{(n+1)(j-1)},\sigma({b}_{(n+1)j},\ldots,{b}_{(n+1)(j+m-1)}),{b}_{(n+1)(j+m)},\ldots,{b}_{(n+1)k_{n+1}}]
.

(e)

For every
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,j-1\,\}
,
{b}_{n\ell}={b}_{(n+1)\ell}
and
for every
\ell\in\{j+1,\ldots,k_{n}\,\}
,
{b}_{n\ell}={b}_{(n+1)(\ell+m-1)}
.

(f)

In the last reduction step of (a’) we apply the rule
\sigma({b}_{(n+1)j},\ldots,{b}_{(n+1)(j+m-1)})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}_{nj}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
.

By Condition (
\mathrm{a}^{\prime}
),
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
reaches
{d}

from a proper extension of
{b}_{nj}
. By Condition (
\mathrm{b}^{\prime}
),
for every
\ell\in\{\,j,\ldots,j+m-1\,\}
,
{c}_{(n+1)\ell}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
and
{c}_{(n+1)\ell}\subseteq{b}_{n\ell}
.
We assumed that
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
simulates the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

on the constant
{d}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
Therefore by the rewrite rule appearing in Condition (f) there exists a rewrite rule

\sigma({c}_{(n+1)j},\ldots,{c}_{(n+1)(j+m-1)})\dot{\rightarrow}{c}
in
R\langle E,F\rangle
with
{c}\subseteq{b}_{nj}
.

For every
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,j-1\,\}
, let
{c}_{n\ell}={c}_{(n+1)\ell}
,

let
{c}_{nj}={c}
,

for every
\ell\in\{j+1,\ldots,k_{n}\,\}
, let
{c}_{n\ell}={c}_{(n+1)(\ell+j-1)}
.

Thence

for every
\ell\in\{\,1,\ldots,k_{n}\,\}
,

{c}_{n\ell}\subseteq{b}_{n\ell}
and

\delta_{n+1}[{c}_{(n+1)1},\ldots,{c}_{(n+1)k_{n+1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}\delta_{n}[{c}_{n1},\ldots,{c}_{nk_{n}}]
.

By the sequence of reductions (
\mathrm{a}^{\prime}
) we have

\delta_{n}[{b}_{n1},\ldots,{b}_{nk_{n}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{n-1}[{b}_{(n-1)1},\ldots,{b}_{(n-1)k_{n-1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\cdots\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{2}[{b}_{21},\ldots,{b}_{2k_{2}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\newline
\delta_{1}[{b}_{11},\ldots,{b}_{1k_{1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}{d}
.

Consequently by the induction hypothesis,

for any
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,n-1\,\}
and
\ell\in\{\,1,\ldots,k_{i}\,\}
, there exist
{c}_{i\ell}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
,
with
{c}_{i\ell}\subseteq{b}_{i\ell}
, and

there exists

{e}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
with
{e}\subseteq{d}

such that the following sequence of reductions holds:

\delta_{n}[{c}_{n1},\ldots,{c}_{nk_{n}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}\delta_{n-1}[{c}_{(n-1)1},\ldots,{c}_{(n-1)k_{n-1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}\cdots\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}\delta_{1}[{c}_{11},\ldots,{c}_{1k_{1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}{e}
.

Then we have

\delta_{n+1}[{c}_{(n+1)1},\ldots,{c}_{(n+1)k_{n+1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}\delta_{n}[{c}_{n1},\ldots,{c}_{nk_{n}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}\delta_{n-1}[{c}_{(n-1)1},\ldots,{c}_{(n-1)k_{n-1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}\newline
\cdots\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}\delta_{1}[{c}_{11},\ldots,{c}_{1k_{1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}{e}
.∎

Lemma
10.107
implies the following lemma.

Lemma 10.108
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
such that GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
simulates the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

on a constant
{d}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
. Let
s\in T_{\Sigma}
and
s\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}^{*}{d}
.
Then
there exists
{e}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
with
{e}\subseteq{d}

such that
s\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}^{*}{e}
.

Lemma 10.109
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
.

For each
t\in W
,

\left(t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}}\mbox{ or }t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}}\right)

if and only if
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.

Proof.

(\Rightarrow)
Let

t\in W
. Then by our assumption,

t/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}=t/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}\mbox{ or }t/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}=t/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle}
.

Assume that
t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}}
. The case
t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}}

is similar. We now show that GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
simulates the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
on
t/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.
To this end, let
{d}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
be such that

R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
reaches
t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}
from a proper extension of the constant
{d}
.
Then there exists
\delta\in CON_{\Sigma,1}

such that
\delta[{d}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}^{*}t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}
.
Let

\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
be a rewrite rule with
{b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m}\in C\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
, and let
{c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{m}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
with

{c}_{i}\subseteq{b}_{i}
for all
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,m\,\}
. We now show that
there exists a rewrite rule in
R\langle E,F\rangle
with left-hand side

\sigma({c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{m})
.
Let
t_{1}\in{c}_{1},\ldots,t_{m}\in{c}_{m}
. By Definition
4.27
, we have

t_{1},\ldots,t_{m}\in W
,

t_{1}/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}={c}_{1},\ldots,t_{m}/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}={c}_{m}
,

t_{1}/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}={b}_{1},\ldots,t_{m}/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}={b}_{m}
.

Hence

\sigma(t_{1}/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle},\ldots,t_{m}/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
.

Then by Proposition
4.33
,

\delta[\sigma(t_{1},\ldots,t_{m})]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}^{*}\delta[\sigma({b}_{1},\ldots,{b}_{m})]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta[{d}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}^{*}t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}
.

By Proposition
4.35
,

\delta[\sigma(t_{1},\ldots,t_{m})]\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}t
.
By the equation
t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
, we have

\delta[\sigma(t_{1},\ldots,t_{m})]\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}t
.
By Proposition
4.38
,

\delta[\sigma(t_{1},\ldots,t_{m})]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}^{*}t/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
.

By Proposition
4.32
,

R\langle E,F\rangle
is convergent. Observe that
t/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
is irreducible for
R\langle E,F\rangle
.
Thence we have

\delta[\sigma(t_{1},\ldots,t_{m})]{\downarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}=t/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
.

By the set memberships
t_{1},\ldots,t_{m}\in W
and by Proposition
4.33
, we have

t_{j}\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}^{*}t_{j}/_{\Theta\langle{E},F\rangle}
for every
j\in\{\,1,\ldots,m\,\}
.
Then

\delta[\sigma(t_{1},\ldots,t_{m})]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}^{*}\delta[\sigma(t_{1}/_{\Theta\langle{E},F\rangle},\ldots,t_{m}/_{\Theta\langle{E},F\rangle})]
.

By Proposition
4.32
,

R\langle E,F\rangle
is convergent. Thence we have

\delta[\sigma(t_{1}/_{\Theta\langle{E},F\rangle},\ldots,t_{m}/_{\Theta\langle{E},F\rangle})]{\downarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}=t/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
.

Therefore, there exists
a rewrite rule with left-hand side
\sigma(t_{1}/_{\Theta\langle{E},F\rangle},\ldots,t_{m}/_{\Theta\langle{E},F\rangle})

in
R\langle{E},F\rangle
.

(\Leftarrow)
Let
t\in W
. We assumed that GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
. By Definition
10.101
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
or
GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

on the constant
t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
Assume that GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
simulates the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

on
t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}
. Then
t/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}=t/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
.
Let
s\in t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}
.
Consequently by Proposition
4.38
,

s\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,E\rangle}^{*}t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}
.
By Lemma
10.108
, there exists
{e}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
such that
{e}\subseteq t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}
and

s\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}^{*}{e}
.
Then
{e}=p/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
for some
p\in W
.
By Proposition
4.35
,

s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}t
and
s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}p
.
Then
s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}p
. Thus

{e}=p/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}\subseteq t/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}=t/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
. Consequently

{e}=t/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
.
Hence

s\in t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
.
Consequently,
for any
s\in t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}
, we have

s\in t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
.
Thus

t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}\subseteq t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
, and hence
t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
.
We showed that if GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
simulates the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

on
t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}
, then
t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
.

We can show similarly that if GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
simulates the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

on
t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}
, then
t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}
.
Consequently,

for each

t\in W
,

\left(t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}\mbox{ or }t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}\right)
. ∎

Example 10.110
.

We continue Example
3.22
and its sequels, Examples
5.55
,
7.74
.
There does not exist a path of positive length in
AUX[E;F]
. There exists one cycle in
AUX[E;F]
. The cycle is of length
1
, it starts from the vertex

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
, and leads to
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.
By Lemma
6.71
,

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
reaches
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}

from a proper extension of

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.
Recall that

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{false}
and

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{false}
and

\#/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{false}
.
Then the formula

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}
or
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}

does not hold.
Thence the following statement does not hold:
for each
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,

1.

{b}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}
or
{b}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}

and

2.

if

{b}\not\in C\langle E,F\rangle
, then
for each
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
if there is a path of positive length or a cycle from

{b}
to
{c}

in the auxiliary dpwpa

AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
, then

{c}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}
.

By Lemma
10.104
, GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together do not simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.

Example 10.111
.

We continue
Example
3.23
, and its sequels, Examples

, and
7.75
.
There exist one path of positive length and two cycles in
AUX[E;F]
.

The first cycle: Its length is
1
, and it starts from the vertex

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
and leads to
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
. By Lemma
6.71
,

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
reaches

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
from a proper extension of
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.
Recall that

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{false}
,

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}
, and

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}
.

The path of positive length: Its length is
1
, and it starts from the vertex

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
and leads to
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
. By Lemma
6.71
,

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
reaches
\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
from a proper extension of

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
. Recall that

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}
,

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{false}
, and

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}
.

The second cycle: Its length is
1
, and it starts from the vertex

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
and leads to

. By Lemma

,

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
reaches
\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}

from a proper extension of

. Recall that

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}
,

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{false}
, and

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{false}
.

Observe that the following statement holds:
for each

,

or

and

if there is a path of positive length or a cycle from

.

By Lemma
