, GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.

11
Main Cases of the Decision Algorithm

We now recall the four main cases of our decision algorithm. We show that we can decide in
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
time which main cases hold.

Main Case 1:

\Sigma
is a unary signature.

Main Case 2:

Both GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
are total.

Main Case 3:

\Sigma_{k}\neq\emptyset
for some
k\geq 2
, and at least one of
R\langle E,F\rangle

and
R\langle F,E\rangle
is total.

Main Case 4:

\Sigma
has a symbol of arity at least
2
,
and GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
are not total.

Recall that Main Case 2 overlaps with Main Case 1 and Main Case 3, and is a subcase of the union of
Main Case 1 and Main Case 3.

Lemma 11.112
.

Let
E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
.
We can decide which Main Cases hold in
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
time.

Proof.

We run Algorithm CAD on
\Sigma
,
E
, and
F
.
By Proposition
4.48
, we get the following output in
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
time:

1.

the relations
\rho\langle E,F\rangle
,
\rho\langle F,E\rangle
, and
\rho\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
;

2.

the set of nullary symbols
C\langle E,F\rangle
,
C\langle F,E\rangle
, and

C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
;

3.

INC(E\cup F,E)
stored in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT1
;

4.

INC(E\cup F,F)
stored in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT2
;

5.

NUM(E\cup F,E)
stored in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT3
;

6.

NUM(E\cup F,F)
stored in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT4
;

7.

the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
stored in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT5
;
each node in
RBT5
contains a search key which is a rule in
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, and satellite data
counter_{E}
and
counter_{F}
;

8.

BSTEP\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
stored in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT6
;
for every ordered pair
({b},{c})\in BSTEP\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, the search key is the first component
{b}
, the second component,
{c}
is satellite data;

9.

the GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
stored in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT7
;

10.

the GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
stored in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT8
;

11.

the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
for the GTESs
E
and
F
, and
for every vertex
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
the values of the attributes
{b}.equal_{E}
,
{b}.equal_{F}
,
{b}.keeps_{E}
, and
{b}.keeps_{F}
.

By Lemma
4.50
, we decide in
O(n)
time whether

R\langle E,F\rangle
is total, and whether

R\langle F,E\rangle
is total.
Then we decide in a total of
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
time which Main Cases hold.
∎

We study Main Cases 1, 2, 3, and 4 one by one in Sections
12
,
13
,
14
, and
15
, respectively.
First we consider Main Case 1.

12
Main Case 1

In this main case,
\Sigma
is a unary signature, and
E
and
F
are GTESs over
\Sigma
.
We define the Algorithm Nested Partial Depth First Search (NPDFS for short)
on the Auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]
for the GTESs
E
and
F
. NPDFS runs in
O(n^{2})
time. Furthermore,
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
if and only if
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle)
if and only if
NPDFS outputs true. Thus,
we can decide in
O(n^{2})
time whether

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
. First we note that
we can also apply the decision algorithm of Champavère et al
[
10
]
.

Lemma
9.93
, Proposition
9.94
, and Proposition
9.92
imply the following theorem.

Theorem 12.113
.

Let
E
and
F
be GTESs over a unary signature
\Sigma
.
We can decide in
O(n^{3})
time whether

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}=\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

We present a faster and simpler algorithm for solving our decidability problem.
We assume that we have already run Algorithm CAD on
\Sigma
,
E
, and
F
, and have obtained all the output it produces, see Proposition
4.48
.
Example
3.18
and its sequel, Example

5.51
, furthermore Example
3.19
and its sequel,
5.52

illustrate this main case.

Lemma 12.114
.

Let
E
and
F
be GTESs over a unary signature
\Sigma
.
Let
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
be
such that GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
simulates the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
on the constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
. Assume that Conditions (a) and (b) hold:

(a)

\delta_{n}[{b}_{n1},\ldots,{b}_{nk_{n}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{n-1}[{b}_{(n-1)1},\ldots,{b}_{(n-1)k_{n-1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{2}[{b}_{21},\ldots,{b}_{2k_{2}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\cdots\newline
\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{2}[{b}_{21},\ldots,{b}_{2k_{2}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{1}[{b}_{11},\ldots,{b}_{1k_{1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}{b}
,

where

n\geq 1
and
for every
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,n\,\}
,

\left(k_{i}\in{\mathbb{N}},\;\delta_{i}\in CON_{\Sigma,k_{i}},\mbox{ and }{b}_{i1},\ldots,{b}_{ik_{i}}\in C\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle\right)
.

(b)

{c}_{n1},\ldots,{c}_{nk_{n}}\in C\langle E,F\rangle

such that
{c}_{ni}\subseteq{b}_{ni}
for every
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,k_{n}\,\}
.

Then there exists
{c}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
with
{c}\subseteq{b}
and for every
j\in\{\,1,\ldots,n\,\}
,
there exists
{c}_{j1},\ldots,{c}_{jk_{j}}\in C\langle E,F\rangle

such that
{c}_{ji}\subseteq{b}_{ji}
for every
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,k_{j}\,\}
, and the following sequence of reductions holds:

\delta_{n}[{c}_{n1},\ldots,{c}_{nk_{n}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{n-1}[{c}_{(n-1)1},\ldots,{c}_{(n-1)k_{n-1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{2}[{c}_{21},\ldots,{c}_{2k_{2}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\cdots\newline
\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{2}[{c}_{21},\ldots,{c}_{2k_{2}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{1}[{c}_{11},\ldots,{c}_{1k_{1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}{c}
,

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
n
.

Base Case:

n=1
. Then
\delta_{1}=\sigma(\diamondsuit,\ldots,\diamondsuit)
for some
\sigma\in\Sigma_{k_{1}}
,

\delta_{1}[{b}_{11},\ldots,{k_{1}}]=\sigma({b}_{11},\ldots,{k_{1}})
, and

\sigma({b}_{11},\ldots,{b}_{1k_{1}})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
.
We assumed that GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
simulates the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

on the constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
Hence by Condition (b), and the rewrite rule above there exists a rewrite rule

\sigma({c}_{11},\ldots,{c}_{1k_{1}})\dot{\rightarrow}{c}\in R\langle E,F\rangle

for some
{c}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
with
{c}\subseteq{b}
.
Then we have

\delta_{1}[{c}_{11},\ldots,{c}_{1k_{1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}{c}
.

Induction Step:
Let
n\geq 1
, and assume that the statement of the lemma is true for
n
. We now show
that it is also true for
n+1
as well.
Assume that Conditions
(\mathrm{a}^{\prime})
and
(\mathrm{b}^{\prime})
hold:

(
\mathrm{a}^{\prime}
)

\delta_{n+1}[{b}_{(n+1)1},\ldots,{b}_{(n+1)k_{(n+1)}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{n}[{b}_{n1},\ldots,{b}_{nk_{n}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{n-1}[{b}_{(n-1)1},\ldots,{b}_{(n-1)k_{n-1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\newline
\cdots\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{2}[{b}_{21},\ldots,{b}_{2k_{2}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{1}[{b}_{11},\ldots,{b}_{1k_{1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}{b}
,

where

n\geq 1
and
for every
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,n+1\,\}
,

\left(k_{i}\in{\mathbb{N}},\;\delta_{i}\in CON_{\Sigma,k_{i}},\mbox{ and }{b}_{i1},\ldots,{b}_{ik_{i}}\in C\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle\right)
.

(
\mathrm{b}^{\prime}
)

{c}_{(n+1)1},\ldots,{c}_{(n+1)k_{n+1}}\in C\langle E,F\rangle

such that
{c}_{(n+1)i}\subseteq{b}_{(n+1)i}
for every
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,k_{n+1}\,\}
.

Then we obtain

\delta_{n+1}
from
\delta_{n}
replacing the
\ell
th occurrence from left-to right of
\diamondsuit
with the term
\sigma(\diamondsuit,\ldots,\diamondsuit)
for some
\sigma\in\Sigma_{m}
,

m\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Consequently,

k_{n+1}-m+1=k_{n}
,

\delta_{n+1}[{b}_{(n+1)1},\ldots,{b}_{(n+1)k_{(n+1)}}]=\delta_{n}[{b}_{(n+1)1},\ldots,{b}_{(n+1)(\ell-1)},\sigma({b}_{(n+1)\ell},\ldots,{b}_{(n+1)(\ell+m)}),{b}_{(n+1)(\ell+1)},\ldots{b}_{(n+1)k_{(n+1)}}]
,

\delta_{n}[{b}_{n1},\ldots,{b}_{nk_{n}}]=\delta_{n}[{b}_{(n+1)1},\ldots,{b}_{(n+1)(\ell-1)},{b}_{n,\ell},{b}_{(n+1)(\ell+1)},\ldots{b}_{(n+1)k_{(n+1)}}]
,

{b}_{n1}={b}_{(n+1)1},\ldots,{b}_{n(\ell-1)}={b}_{(n+1)(\ell-1)},{b}_{n(\ell+1)}={b}_{(n+1)(\ell-m+1)},\ldots,{b}_{nk_{n}}={b}_{(n+1)k_{(n+1)}}

and

\sigma({b}_{(n+1)\ell},\ldots,{b}_{(n+1)(\ell+m)})\dot{\rightarrow}{b}_{n\ell}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle.

(1)

We assumed that
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
simulates the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

on the constant
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
By Condition (
\mathrm{a}^{\prime}
),
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
reaches
{b}

from a proper extension of
{b}_{n\ell}
. Therefore by the rewrite rule (
1
) and Condition (
\mathrm{b}^{\prime}
),
there exists a rewrite rule

\sigma({c}_{(n+1)\ell},\ldots,{c}_{(n+1)(\ell+m)})\dot{\rightarrow}{c}_{n\ell}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle.

(2)

for some
{c}_{n\ell}\in C\langle E\cup F,E\rangle
with
{c}_{n\ell}\subseteq{b}_{n\ell}
.
Let

{c}_{n1}={c}_{(n+1)1},\ldots,{c}_{n(l-1)}={c}_{(n+1)(l-1)},\ldots,{c}_{n(l+1)}={c}_{(n+1)(l-m+1)},\ldots,{c}_{nk_{n}}={c}_{(n+1)k_{(n+1)}}

By the induction hypothesis,
there exists
{c}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
with
{c}\subseteq{b}
such that the following sequence of reductions holds:

\delta_{n}[{c}_{n1},\ldots,{c}_{nk_{n}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{n-1}[{c}_{(n-1)1},\ldots,{c}_{(n-1)k_{n-1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\cdots\newline
\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{2}[{c}_{21},\ldots,{c}_{2k_{2}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{1}[{c}_{11},\ldots,{c}_{1k_{1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}{c}
,

In this way

\delta_{n+1}[{c}_{(n+1)1},\ldots,{c}_{(n+1)k_{(}n+1)}]=\newline
\delta_{n}[{c}_{(n+1)1},\ldots,{c}_{(n+1)(\ell-1)},\sigma({c}_{(n+1)\ell},\ldots,{c}_{(n+1)(\ell+m)}),{c}_{(n+1)(\ell+1)},\ldots{c}_{(n+1)k_{(n+1)}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\newline
\delta_{n}[{c}_{(n+1)1},\ldots,{c}_{(n+1)(\ell-1)},{c}_{n,\ell},{c}_{(n+1)(\ell+1)},\ldots{c}_{(n+1)k_{(n+1)}}]=\delta_{n}[{c}_{n1},\ldots,{c}_{nk_{n}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\newline
\delta_{n-1}[{c}_{(n-1)1},\ldots,{c}_{(n-1)k_{n-1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\cdots\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{2}[{c}_{21},\ldots,{c}_{2k_{2}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta_{1}[{c}_{11},\ldots,{c}_{1k_{1}}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}{c}
.
∎

Lemma 12.115
.

Let
E
and
F
be GTESs over a unary signature
\Sigma
.

For each

t\in W
,

\left(t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}\mbox{ or }t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}\right)

if and only if
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.

Proof.

(\Rightarrow)

Let
t\in W
. Then by our assumption,
t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
or

t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}
. Assume that
t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
. The case
t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}
is similar.
Let
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
be such that

R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
reaches
t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}
from a proper extension of the constant
{c}
. Then there exists
\delta\in CON_{\Sigma,1}

such that
\delta[{c}]{\downarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}=t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}
. Consider any rewrite rule

\sigma({c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{m})\dot{\rightarrow}{c}
in
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
with right-hand side
{c}
.
We now distinguish two cases depending on the value of
m
.

Case 1:

m=1
. Then we consider the rewrite rule

\sigma({c}_{1})\dot{\rightarrow}{c}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
. Then let

{d}_{1}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
with

{d}_{1}\subseteq{c}_{1}
, and let
t_{1}\in T_{\Sigma}
such that
t_{1}\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}^{*}{d}_{1}
.
Then we have

\delta[\sigma(t_{1})]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}^{*}\delta[\sigma({c}_{1})]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta[{c}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}^{*}t/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.

By Proposition
4.35
,
\delta[\sigma(t_{1})]\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}t
.
As a consequence, by our assumption that
t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
, we have

\delta[\sigma(t_{1})]\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}t
. Consequently, by Proposition
4.38
,

\delta[\sigma(t_{1})]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}^{*}t/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
.

Hence

t_{1}\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}^{*}{d}_{1}
,

\sigma(t_{1})\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}^{*}\sigma({d}_{1})\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}{d}
for some
{d}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
,
where the rewrite rule
\sigma({d}_{1})\dot{\rightarrow}{d}
is in
R\langle E,F\rangle
,
and

\delta[\sigma(t_{1})]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}^{*}\delta[\sigma({d}_{1})]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}^{*}\delta[{d}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}^{*}t/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
.

Case 2:

m=0
. Then we consider the rewrite rule

\sigma\dot{\rightarrow}{c}\in R\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle
.
Then we have

\delta[\sigma]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}\delta[{c}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}^{*}t/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.

By Proposition
4.35
,
\delta[\sigma]\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}t
.
As a consequence, by our assumption that
t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
, we have

\delta[\sigma]\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}t
. Consequently, by Proposition
4.38
,

\delta[\sigma]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}^{*}t/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
.

Therefore

\sigma\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}{d}
for some
{d}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
,
where the rewrite rule
\sigma\dot{\rightarrow}{d}
is in
R\langle E,F\rangle
,
and

\delta[\sigma]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}\delta[{d}]\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}^{*}t/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
.

(\Leftarrow)
Let
t\in W
and
s\in t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}
.
Then

s\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}^{*}t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}
. We assumed that GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
. Consequently,

R\langle E,F\rangle
or
R\langle F,E\rangle

simulate
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
on the constant
t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}
. Assume that
R\langle E,F\rangle

simulates
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
on the constant
t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}
. The case
R\langle F,E\rangle

simulates
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
on the constant
t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}
is similar.
By Lemma
12.114
, there exists
{e}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
with
{e}\subseteq t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}
such that
s\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}^{*}{e}
.
By Proposition
4.39
,
{e}=t/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
.
For this reason

s\in t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
.
Consequently,
for any
s\in t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}
, we have

s\in t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
.
As a consequence

t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}\subseteq t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
, and hence
t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
.
∎

12.1
Nested Partial Depth First Search on the Auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]
for the GTESs
E
and
F

Algorithm Nested Partial Depth First Search (NPDFS for short) on the Auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]
for the GTESs
E
and
F
implements a nested loop.
Algorithm NPDFS calls the function NPDFS-VISIT, and in this way it implements the outer loop, which is
a prefix of a depth first search on
AUX[E;F]
,
and can terminate before visiting all vertices of
AUX[E;F]
in case that GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together do not simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.

1.

Algorithm NPDFS calls the functions INNER-LOOP-E, INNER-LOOP-F, and
NPDFS-VISIT.

2.

Function NPDFS-VISIT calls the function INNER-LOOP-E, INNER-LOOP-F, and
NPDFS-VISIT.

In this way, algorithm NPDFS decides the following formula:
For each
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,

1.

{b}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}
and
for each
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
if there is a path of positive length or a cycle from

{b}
to
{c}

in the auxiliary dpwpa

AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
, then
{c}.keeps_{E}=\mathrm{true}
, or

2.

{b}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}
and
for each
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
if there is a path of positive length or a cycle from

{b}
to
{c}

in the auxiliary dpwpa

AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
, then

{c}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}
.

Function
INNER-LOOP-E is an inner loop inside the body of the outer loop, it
also implements a prefix of a depth first search on those vertices
{c}
which are positive-step reachable
from the current node
{b}
of the outer loop, it can terminate before visiting all vertices
of
AUX[E;F]
which are positive-step
reachable from
{b}
in case that it visits a vertex
{c}
such that
{c}.keeps_{E}=\mathrm{false}
.
It checks whether for each vertex
{c}
which are positive-step reachable
from the current node
{b}
of the outer loop,

{c}.keeps_{E}=\mathrm{true}
.
INNER-LOOP-E decides the following formula:

For each vertex
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
which is positive-step reachable
from the current node
{b}
of the outer loop,
{c}.keeps_{E}=\mathrm{true}
.

Function
INNER-LOOP-F is an inner loop inside the body of the outer loop, it
also implements a prefix of a depth first search on those vertices
{c}
which are positive-step reachable
from the current node
{b}
of the outer loop, it can terminate before visiting all vertices
of
AUX[E;F]
which are positive-step
reachable from
{b}
in case that in case that it visits a vertex
{c}
such that
{c}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{false}
. It checks whether for each vertex
{c}
which are positive-step reachable
from the current node
{b}
of the outer loop,

{c}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}
.
INNER-LOOP-F decides the following formula:

For each vertex
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
which is positive-step reachable
from the current node
{b}
of the outer loop,
{c}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}
.

Definition 12.116
.

Let
\Sigma
be a signature,
E
and
F
be GTESs over
\Sigma
.

Algorithm Nested Partial Depth First Search on Auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]
(NPDFS for short)

Input: the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]
.

Output: Boolean.

{
\mathrm{NPDFS}(AUX[E;F])=\mathrm{true}
if GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, otherwise
\mathrm{NPDFS}(AUX[E;F])=\mathrm{false}
. }

var

{b}:C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
;

⬇

for each
vertex
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

do

begin

{b}.visited\_outer\_loop:=\mathrm{false}

{b}.visited\_inner\_loop_{E}:=\mathrm{false}

{b}.visited\_inner\_loop_{F}:=\mathrm{false}

end

for each
vertex
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

do

if

not

{b}.visited\_outer\_loop

then

begin

{b}.visited\_outer\_loop:=\mathrm{true}

{\bf if}

not
(
{b}.equal_{E}

or

{b}.equal_{F}
)

then

return

\mathrm{false}

{\bf for}

{\bf each}
vertex
{c}\in Adj({b})

do

{\bf if}

not
((
{b}.equal_{E}

and

\mathrm{INNER-LOOP-E}(AUX[E;F],{c})

and

(\mathrm{NPDFS-VISIT}(AUX[E;F],{c})

or
(
{b}.equal_{F}

and

\mathrm{INNER-LOOP-F}(AUX[E;F],{c})

and

(\mathrm{NPDFS-VISIT}(AUX[E;F],{c}))

then

return

\mathrm{false}

end

output

(\mathrm{true})

function

\mathrm{NPDFS-VISIT}(AUX[E;F],{b}):\;\mathrm{Boolean}

if

not

{b}.visited\_outer\_loop

then

begin

{b}.visited\_outer\_loop:=\mathrm{true}

{\bf if}

not
(
{b}.equal_{E}

or

{b}.equal_{F}
)

then

return

\mathrm{false}

{\bf for}

{\bf each}
vertex
{c}\in Adj({b})

do

{\bf if}

not
((
{b}.equal_{E}

and

\mathrm{INNER-LOOP-E}(AUX[E;F],{c})

and

(\mathrm{NPDFS-VISIT}(AUX[E;F],{c})

or
(
{b}.equal_{F}

and

\mathrm{INNER-LOOP-F}(AUX[E;F],{c})

and

(\mathrm{NPDFS-VISIT}(AUX[E;F],{c}))

then

return

\mathrm{false}

end

output

(\mathrm{true})

function

\mathrm{INNER-LOOP-E}(AUX[E;F],{b}):\;\mathrm{Boolean}

{b}.visited\_inner\_loop_{E}:=\mathrm{true}
;

if

{b}.keeps_{E}

{\bf then}

for

each
vertex
{c}\in Adj({b})

do

{\bf if}

not

\mathrm{INNER-LOOP-E}(AUX[E;F],{c})

{\bf then}\;{\bf begin}

{b}.visited\_inner\_loop_{E}:=\mathrm{false}
;

{\bf return}\;\mathrm{false}

end

{\bf else}\;{\bf begin}

{b}.visited\_inner\_loop_{E}:=\mathrm{false}
;

{\bf return}\;\mathrm{false}

end

{b}.visited\_inner\_loop_{E}:=\mathrm{false}
;

{\bf output}(\mathrm{true})

function

\mathrm{INNER-LOOP-F}(AUX[E;F],{b}):\;\mathrm{Boolean}

{b}.visited\_inner\_loop_{F}:=\mathrm{true}
;

if

{b}.keeps_{F}

{\bf then}

for

each
vertex
{c}\in Adj({b})

do

{\bf if}

not

\mathrm{INNER-LOOP-F}(AUX[E;F],{c})

{\bf then}\;{\bf begin}

{b}.visited\_inner\_loop_{F}:=\mathrm{false}
;

{\bf return}\;\mathrm{false}

end

{\bf else}\;{\bf begin}

{b}.visited\_inner\_loop_{F}:=\mathrm{false}
;

{\bf return}\;\mathrm{false}

end

{b}.visited\_inner\_loop_{F}:=\mathrm{false}
;

{\bf output}(\mathrm{true})

By direct inspection of Definition
12.116
, we get the following result.

Lemma 12.117
.

Let
E
and
F
be GTESs over a unary signature
\Sigma
.
NPDFS outputs true on
AUX[E;F]
if and only if
for each
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, Conditions 1 or 2 hold:

1.

{b}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}
and
for each
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
if there is a path of positive length or a cycle from

{b}
to
{c}

in the auxiliary dpwpa

AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
, then
{c}.keeps_{E}=\mathrm{true}
.

2.

{b}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}
and
for each
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
if there is a path of positive length or a cycle from

{b}
to
{c}

in the auxiliary dpwpa

AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
, then

{c}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}
.

We get the following lemma by direct inspection of Definition
12.116
.

Lemma 12.118
.

Let
E
and
F
be GTESs over a unary signature
\Sigma
.

If GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, then Conditions 1–3 hold:

1.

Algorithm NPDFS on
AUX[E;F]
in the outer loop visits once each vertex
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.

2.

For each vertex
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
visited in the outer loop,

(b)

{b}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}
and
for each
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
if there is a path of positive length or a cycle from

{c}
to
{b}

in the auxiliary dpwpa

AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
, then
{c}.keeps_{E}=\mathrm{true}
, or

(c)

{b}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}
and
for each
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
if there is a path of positive length or a cycle from

{c}
to
{b}

in the auxiliary dpwpa

AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
, then

{c}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}
.

3.

Algorithm NPDFS on
AUX[E;F]
outputs true.

If GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together do not simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, then Conditions 4–6 hold:

4.

Algorithm NPDFS on
AUX[E;F]
visits at most once each vertex
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
in the outer loop.

5.

There exists a vertex
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
such that Algorithm NPDFS on
AUX[E;F]

visits
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
in the outer loop, and Conditions 2.(a) and 2.(b) are false for
{b}
.

6.

Algorithm NPDFS on
AUX[E;F]
outputs false.

Example 12.119
.

We now continue
Example
3.18
, Example
5.51
, Example
7.72
, and Example
10.105
.
We run Algorithm NPDFS on
AUX[E;F]
. Then Algorithm NPDFS outputs true.
By the last line of Example
10.105
, GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
Consequently Algorithm NPDFS outputs the answer to the question whether GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.

Example 12.120
.

We now continue Examples
3.19
,
5.52
,
7.73
, and
10.106
.
We run Algorithm NPDFS on
AUX[E;F]
.
Then Algorithm NPDFS outputs false. By the last lines of Example
10.106
, GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together do not simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
Thus Algorithm NPDFS outputs the answer to the question whether GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.

Lemma 12.121
.

[
13
]
Let
E
and
F
be GTESs over a unary signature
\Sigma
.
Algorithm NPDFS runs on
AUX[E;F]
in
O(n^{2})
time.

Proof.

Recall that
n=\mathrm{size}(E)+\mathrm{size}(F)
.
By Statement
4.28
,

|C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle|\leq n
. By Statement
6.64
,

|A[E;F]|\leq 2n
.
The running time of depth first search on
AUX[E;F]
is
O(|C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle|+|A[E;F]|)

[
13
, Section 22.3]
. Consequently, the running time of depth first search on
AUX[E;F]
is
O(n)
.

The outer loop of
Algorithm NPDFS on
AUX[E;F]
implements a prefix of
the depth first search on
AUX[E;F]

[
13
]
, it can terminate before visiting all vertices of
AUX[E;F]
.
Consequently, the outer loop on
AUX[E;F]
takes
O(n)
time.

Function INNER-LOOP-E on
AUX[E;F]
and any vertex
{b}

implements a prefix of a depth first search on
AUX[E;F]
starting from
{b}
.
Accordingly the running time of function INNER-LOOP-E is
O(n)
.
Similarly, the running time of function INNER-LOOP-F is

O(n)
.
By Statement
4.28
,
|C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle|\leq n
, therefore
INNER-LOOP-E and INNER-LOOP-F run at most
n
times. Accordingly, Algorithm NPDFS runs on
AUX[E;F]
in
O(n^{2})
time.
∎

12.2
Decision Algorithm and its verification

We present our decision algorithm and show its correctnes.

Lemma 12.122
.

Let
E
and
F
be GTESs over a unary signature
\Sigma
.
Then the following conditions are pairwise equivalent:

(i)

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
,

(ii)

For each
t\in W
,

(t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}\mbox{ or }t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}})
.

(iii)

GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.

(iv)

For each
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
or GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

on the constant
{b}
.

(v)

For each
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,

(a)

{b}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}
and
for each
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
if there is a path of positive length or a cycle from

{b}
to
{c}

in the auxiliary dpwpa

AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
, then
{c}.keeps_{E}=\mathrm{true}
, or

(b)

{b}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}
and
for each
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
if there is a path of positive length or a cycle from

{b}
to
{c}

in the auxiliary dpwpa

AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
, then

{c}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}
.

(vi)

NPDFS(AUX[E;F])=\mathrm{true}
.

Proof.

\mathrm{(i)}\Leftrightarrow\mathrm{(ii)}
: By Lemma
9.93
.

\mathrm{(ii)}\Leftrightarrow\mathrm{(iii)}
: By Lemma
12.115
.

\mathrm{(iii)}\Leftrightarrow\mathrm{(iv)}
: By Definition
10.101
.

\mathrm{(iv)}\Leftrightarrow\mathrm{(v)}
: By Definition
10.98
and Definition

6.59
and Lemma
8.77
and Lemma
6.71
.

\mathrm{(v)}\Leftrightarrow\mathrm{(vi)}
: By Lemma
12.117
.
∎

Lemma 12.123
.

Let
E
and
F
be GTESs over a unary signature
\Sigma
.
Then we can decide in
O(n^{2})
time whether

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

Proof.

Recall that we have run Algorithm CAD on
\Sigma
,
E
, and
F
.
By Proposition
4.48
, we have obtained in
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
time
the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
for the GTESs
E
and
F
, and
for every vertex
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
the values of the attributes
{b}.equal_{E}
,
{b}.equal_{F}
,
{b}.keeps_{E}
, and
{b}.keeps_{F}
.

3. We run Algorithm NPDFS on
AUX[E;F]
in
O(n^{2})
time by Lemma
12.121
. Then by Lemma
12.122
,
NPDFS outputs true if and only if

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

Summing up the time complexities of the three steps above, we get that the time complexity of our entire computation is
O(n^{2})
.
∎

Example 12.124
.

We now continue
Examples
3.18
,
5.51
,
7.72
,
10.105
. and
12.119
.
Recall that we run Algorithm NPDFS on the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]
, and Algorithm NPDFS outputs true to the question whether
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
Hence Condition (iii) in Lemma
12.122
holds.
Consequently, by Lemma
12.122
,

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

Example 12.125
.

We now continue Examples
3.19
,
5.52
,
7.73
,
10.106
, and
12.120
.
Recall that we run Algorithm NPDFS on the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]
, and Algorithm NPDFS outputs false to the question whether
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
For this reason Condition (iii) in Lemma
12.122
does not hold.
Consequently, by Lemma
12.122
,

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\not\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

13
Main Case 2

In this main case,
E
and
F
are GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
such that
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
are total.
We assume that we have already run Algorithm CAD on
\Sigma
,
E
, and
F
, and have obtained all the output it produces, see Proposition
4.48
.
We showed that
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}

if and only if each
\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
equivalence class is equal to a

\Theta\langle E,F\rangle
equivalence class or a
\Theta\langle F,E\rangle
equivalence class.
By direct inspection of

INC(E\cup F,E)
stored in a red-black tree and

INC(E\cup F,F)
stored in a red-black tree
we decide in
O(n)

time whether each
\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
equivalence class is equal to a

\Theta\langle E,F\rangle
equivalence class or a
\Theta\langle F,E\rangle
equivalence class. Thence we decide in

O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
time whether

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.
Example
3.20
and its sequels, Examples
5.53
and
13.127
, in addition
Example
3.21
and its sequels, Examples
5.54
and
13.128

exemplify this case.
First we note that
we can also apply the decision algorithm of Champavère et al
[
10
]
.
Lemma
9.97
and Proposition
9.94
, and Proposition
9.92
imply the following theorem.

Theorem 13.126
.

Let
E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
such that
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
are total.
We can decide in
O(n^{3})
time whether

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}=\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

We present a faster and simpler algorithm for solving our decidability problem.

Example 13.127
.

We now continue Examples

3.20
and
5.53
. Recall that

1.

both GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
are total,

2.

\#/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}\subset\#/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}
,

3.

\$/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}=\$/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}
,

4.

\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}=\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}
,

5.

\#/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle}=\#/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}
,

6.

\$/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle}\subset\$/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}
,

7.

\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle}=\mathsterling/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}
, and

8.

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

Example 13.128
.

We now continue Examples

3.21
and
5.54
. Recall that

1.

both GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
are total,

2.

the only equivalence class of
\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
is
W
,

3.

\#/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}\subset\#/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}

and
\#/_{\Theta\langle{F},E\rangle}\subset\#/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}
, and

4.

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}\subset\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}
.

Lemma 13.129
.

Let
E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
such that
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
are total.
Then

for each
t\in W
,
\left(t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}\mbox{ or }t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}\right)

if and only if

for each
t\in W
,
\left(t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}=t/_{\Theta\langle{E,F\rangle}}\mbox{ or }t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}=t/_{\Theta\langle{F},E\rangle}\right)
.

Proof.

(
\Rightarrow
)
If

t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
, then by Definition
2.10
,

t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}\cap W\times W=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}\cap W\times W=t/_{\Theta\langle{E,F\rangle}}
.

If

t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}
, then

t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}\cap W\times W=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}\cap W\times W=t/_{\Theta\langle{E,F\rangle}}
.

(
\Leftarrow
)
Let
t\in W
and
s\in t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}
.
By Proposition
4.38
,

s\mathop{\rightarrow}_{R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}^{*}t/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.

As
R\langle E,F\rangle
and
R\langle F,E\rangle

are total, by Lemma
4.42
,

s{\downarrow}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}=p/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}
for some
p\in W
,

and

s{\downarrow}_{R\langle F,E\rangle}=q/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle}
for some

q\in W
.

Consequently,
by Proposition
4.35
,

s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}t
,
s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}p
and
s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}q
.

Therefore
t\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}p
and
t\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}q
.
Therefore,

p\in t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}
and

q\in t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}
.

By our assumption,

t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}=t/_{\Theta\langle{E,F\rangle}}
or
t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}=t/_{\Theta\langle{F},E\rangle})
.

First, suppose that

t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}=t/_{\Theta\langle{E,F\rangle}}
. Therefore, we have

p\in t/_{\Theta\langle{E,F\rangle}}
.
Thus, by
s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}p
, we have

s\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}p\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}t
.
Accordingly,
s\in t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
. By the definition of
s
,

t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}\subseteq t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
.

Since
E\subseteq E\cup F
,
t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}\subseteq t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}
.
Thus,
t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
.

Second, suppose that
t/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}=t/_{\Theta\langle{F,E\rangle}}
. Then by a similar argument as above we can show that

t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}
.
∎

Lemma 13.130
.

Let
E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
such that GTRSs
R\langle E,F\rangle
and
R\langle F,E\rangle
are total.
Then we can decide in
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
time whether

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
9.91
,

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

is total as well. Consequently, by Lemma
9.95
,

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}

if and only if for each
p\in W
,

\left(p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}\mbox{ or }p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}\right)
.

Consequently, by Lemma
13.129
,

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}

if and only if
for each
p\in W
,
\left(p/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}=p_{\Theta\langle{E,F\rangle}}\mbox{ or }p/_{\Theta\langle{E\cup F},\emptyset\rangle}=p/_{\Theta\langle{F},E\rangle}\right)
.

Justified by the above observations and by Statement
6.61
, we compute as follows.
Recall that
NUM(E\cup F,E)
stored in a red-black tree
RBT3
,
and
NUM(E\cup F,F)
stored in a red-black tree
RBT4
.
Alternately in a coordinated way, we traverse the red-black trees
RBT3
and
RBT4

in in-order traversal,
For each
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, we read the pair
({b},k)
, where

k=|\{{c}\in C\langle E,F\rangle\mid{c}\subseteq{b}\,\}|
, and the pair

({b},\ell)
, where

\ell=|\{{c}\in C\langle F,E\rangle\mid{c}\subseteq{b}\,\}|
.
In this way,
we decide in
O(n)

time whether each
\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
equivalence class is equal to a

\Theta\langle E,F\rangle
equivalence class or a
\Theta\langle F,E\rangle
equivalence class, see Definition
4.27
.
If the answer is
“yes”
, then
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
, otherwise,
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\not\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.
∎

14
Main Case 3

In this main case,
E
and
F
are GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
such that

\Sigma
has a symbol of arity at least
2
, and
R\langle E,F\rangle
or
R\langle F,E\rangle
are total.
Without loss of generality, we may assume that
R\langle E,F\rangle
is total from now on
throughout this section.
Example
3.22
and its sequels, Examples
5.55
,
7.74
,
10.110
,

14.135
, and
14.142
plus
Example
3.23
, and its sequels, Examples
5.56
,
7.75
,
10.111
,

14.136
, and

14.143
present this case. We assume that we have already run Algorithm CAD on
\Sigma
,
E
, and
F
, and have obtained all the output it produces, see Proposition
4.48
.
First we note that
we can also apply the decision algorithm of Champavère et al
[
10
]
.

Theorem 14.131
.

Let
\Sigma
be a signature having a symbol of arity at least
2
, and let
E
and
F
be GTESs over
\Sigma
such that
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
or GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
are total.
We can decide in
O(n^{3})
time whether

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}=\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
9.91
,
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
is total as well. Then by Lemma
9.95
and Proposition
9.94
, and Proposition
9.92
we have the result.
∎

We present a faster and simpler decision algorithm.

14.1
Partial Depth First Search on the Auxiliary dpwpa

We now define a partial depth first search on the auxiliary dpwpa

AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
, which is
a restricted and slightly modified verison of the depth first search on graphs
[
13
]
.
We start the partial depth first search
from the elements of
C(E\cup F,\emptyset)\setminus C(E,F)
.

Definition 14.132
.

Let
\Sigma
be a signature such that

\Sigma_{k}\neq\emptyset
for some
k\geq 2
, and let
E
and
F
be GTESs over
\Sigma
.

Algorithm Partial Depth First Search on Auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]
(PDFS for short)

Input: the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]
.
Output: Boolean.

{ Output is true if GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, false otherwise. }

var

{b}:C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
;

⬇

\mathrm{PDFS}(G\langle E,F\rangle):\;\mathrm{Boolean}

for each
vertex
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

do

begin

if

not

{b}.equal_{E}

and

not

{b}.equal_{F}

then

return

\mathrm{false}

{  if
{b}\not\in C\langle E,F\rangle

and

{b}\not\in C\langle F,E\rangle

then GTRS

R\langle E,F\rangle

and GTRS

R\langle F,E\rangle

together do not simulate the GTRS

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
. }

{b}.visited:=\mathrm{false}

end

for each
vertex
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle

do

if

not

{b}.equal_{E}
and
not

{b}.visited

{ if
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle\setminus C(E,F)
and
{b}
has not been visited yet }

{\bf then}

{\bf for}

{\bf each}
vertex
{c}\in Adj({b})

do

{\bf if}

not

{c}.visited

{\bf then}
if
not

\mathrm{PDFS-VISIT}(AUX[E;F],{c})

then

{\bf return}\;\mathrm{false}

{\bf output}(\mathrm{true})

{\bf function}\;\mathrm{PDFS-VISIT}(AUX[E;F],{b}):\;\mathrm{Boolean}

{b}.visited:=\mathrm{true}
;

if

{b}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}

{\bf then}

begin

for

each
vertex
{c}\in Adj({b})

do

{\bf if}

not

{c}.visited

{\bf then}

{\bf if}

{\bf not}

\mathrm{PDFS-VISIT}(AUX[E;F],{c})

{\bf then}

{\bf return}

\mathrm{false}

end

{\bf else}\;{\bf return}\;\mathrm{false}

{\bf output}(\mathrm{true})

When Algorithm PDFS starts the search from a vertex
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle\setminus C(E,F)
, it does not set the attribute
{b}.visited
to true.
Consequently if for this vertex
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle\setminus C(E,F)
, there exists a cycle leading from the vertex
{b}
to itself,
then Algorithm PDFS visits vertex
{b}
twice.

We run Algorithm PDFS on the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]
. We shall show that PDFS on
AUX[E;F]
outputs true if and only if
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.

Lemma 14.133
.

[
13
]
Let
\Sigma
be a signature such that

\Sigma_{k}\neq\emptyset
for some
k\geq 2
, and let

E
and
F
be GTESs over
\Sigma
such that
R\langle E,F\rangle
is total.
Let
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle\setminus C\langle E,F\rangle
.

(i) During the run of Algorithm PDFS on
AUX[E;F]
, Function
\mathrm{PDFS-VISIT}
on
(AUX[E;F],{b})

visits at most once those vertices
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
such that
there is a path of positive length or a cycle from

{b}
to
{c}
.

(ii) During the run of Algorithm PDFS on
AUX[E;F]
, Function
\mathrm{PDFS-VISIT}
visits at most once those vertices
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
such that
there is a path of positive length or a cycle from some vertex

{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle\setminus C\langle E,F\rangle
to
{c}
.

Proof.

We obtain Statement (i) by direct inspection of Definition
14.132
. Statement (i) implies Statement (ii).
∎

Proposition 14.134
.

[
13
]
Let
\Sigma
be a signature such that

\Sigma_{k}\neq\emptyset
for some
k\geq 2
, and let

E
and
F
be GTESs over
\Sigma
such that
R\langle E,F\rangle
is total.
Algorithm PDFS runs on
AUX[E;F]
in
O(n)
time.

Proof.

The running time of
PDFS is
O(|C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle|+|A[E;F]|)

[
13
]
. Recall that
n=\mathrm{size}(E)+\mathrm{size}(F)
.
By Definition
4.27
and Statement
4.46
,

|C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle|\leq|W|\leq n
.
By Statement
6.64

|A[E;F]|\leq 2n
. Hence
|C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle|+|A[E;F]|\leq 3n
. Then
we have the proposition.
∎

Example 14.135
.

We now continue Examples
3.22
,
5.55
,
7.74
, and
10.110
. First
Algorithm PDFS decides whether for each vertex
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, it holds that

{b}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
or

{b}\in C\langle F,E\rangle
, that is,
\left({b}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}\mbox{ or }a.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}\right)
.
Recall that
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{false}
and

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{false}
. As a result

\left(\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{false}\;\,\mbox{and}\;\,\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{false}\right)=\mathrm{true}
.

Consequently, Algorithm PDFS outputs false.

Example 14.136
.

We now continue Examples
3.23
,
5.56
,
7.75
, and
10.111
.
Recall that there exist one path of positive length and two cycles in
AUX[E;F]
.

The first cycle: Its length is
1
, and it starts from the vertex

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
and leads to
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
. By Lemma
6.71
,

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
reaches

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
from a proper extension of the constant
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.
Recall that

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{false}
,

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}
, and

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}
.

The path of positive length: Its length is
1
, and it starts from the vertex

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
and leads to
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
. By Lemma
6.71
,

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
reaches
\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
from a proper extension of the constant

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
. Recall that

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}
,

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{false}
, and

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}
.

The second cycle: Its length is
1
, and it starts from the vertex

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
and leads to
\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
. By Lemma
6.71
,

R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
reaches
\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}

from a proper extension of the constant

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
. Recall that

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}
,

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{false}
, and

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{false}
.

First Algorithm PDFS decides whether for each
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, it holds that

{b}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
or

{b}\in C\langle F,E\rangle
, that is,
\left({b}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}\mbox{ or }a.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}\right)
.
Observe that

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{false}
and

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}
.
Thus

\left(\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}\;\mbox{or}\;\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}\right)=\mathrm{true}
.

Observe that

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}
and

\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{false}
.
As a result

\left(\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}\;\mbox{or}\;\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}\right)=\mathrm{true}
.

Algorithm PDFS examines the vertices of
AUX[E;F]
, it looks for vertices from which it can start the search.
To this end, Algorithm PDFS decides whether for each
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, it holds that

{b}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
or
{b}\in C\langle F,E\rangle
, that is,
\left({b}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}\mbox{ or }a.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}\right)
.
First, Algorithm PDFS examines the vertex
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
. It observes that

\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{false}

and
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}
. Then
Algorithm PDFS starts the search from
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.
It goes along the cycle
(\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle},\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle})
.
Algorithm PDFS observes that
\#/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}
.
Second, Algorithm PDFS examines the vertex
\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.
Since
\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}
, Algorithm PDFS does not start the search starting from
\$/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}
.
Consequently, Algorithm PDFS outputs true.

14.2
Decision Algorithm and its verification

We present our decision algorithm and show its correctnes.

Lemma 14.137
.

Let
\Sigma
be a signature such that

\Sigma_{k}\neq\emptyset
for some
k\geq 2
.
Let
E
and
F
be GTESs over
\Sigma
such that
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
is total.
Then the following conditions are pairwise equivalent:

(i)

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
,

(ii)

for each
t\in W
,

\left(t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}\mbox{ or }t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}}=t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}\right)
.

(iii)

For each
t\in W
,

\left(t/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}=t/_{\Theta\langle E,F\rangle}\mbox{ or }t/_{\Theta\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle}=t/_{\Theta\langle F,E\rangle}\right)
.

(iv)

GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.

(v)

for each
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,

(a)

{b}\in C\langle E,F\rangle
or

{b}\in C\langle F,E\rangle
and

(b)

if

{b}\not\in C\langle E,F\rangle
, then
for each
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
if there is a path of positive length or a cycle from

{b}
to
{c}

in the auxiliary dpwpa

AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
, then
R\langle F,E\rangle
keeps up with
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
writing
{c}
.

(vi)

for each
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,

(a)

{b}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}
or
{b}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}

and

(b)

if

{b}\not\in C\langle E,F\rangle
, then
for each
{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
if there is a path of positive length or a cycle from

{b}
to
{c}

in the auxiliary dpwpa

AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
, then

{c}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}
.

Proof.

\mathrm{(i)}\Leftrightarrow\mathrm{(ii)}
: By Lemma
9.91
and Lemma
9.95
.

\mathrm{(ii)}\Leftrightarrow\mathrm{(iii)}
: By Lemma
9.96
.

\mathrm{(iii)}\Leftrightarrow\mathrm{(iv)}
:
By Lemma
10.109
.

\mathrm{(iv)}\Leftrightarrow\mathrm{(v)}
: By Lemma
10.104
.

\mathrm{(v)}\Leftrightarrow\mathrm{(vi)}
: By Statement 4 in Lemma
8.77
.
∎

We get the following lemma by direct inspection of Definition
14.132
and by the equivalence
\mathrm{(iv)}\Leftrightarrow\mathrm{(vi)}
in Lemma
14.137
.

Lemma 14.138
.

Let
\Sigma
be a signature such that

\Sigma_{k}\neq\emptyset
for some
k\geq 2
, and let

E
and
F
be GTESs over
\Sigma
such that GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
is total.

1. If GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
then Conditions (a) and (b) hold:

(a)

For each vertex
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,

{b}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}
or
{b}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}
, and

(b)

Algorithm PDFS on
AUX[E;F]
visits exactly once each vertex
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
such that there is a path of positive length or a cycle from some vertex

{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle\setminus C\langle E,F\rangle
to
{b}
. For each visited vertex
{b}
,

{b}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{true}
.

(c)

Algorithm PDFS on
AUX[E;F]
outputs true.

2.
If
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together do not simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
then Algorithm PDFS on
AUX[E;F]
outputs false and Conditions (d) or (e) hold:

(d)

There exists a vertex
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
such that

{b}.equal_{E}=\mathrm{true}
or
{b}.equal_{F}=\mathrm{true}
.

(e)

Algorithm PDFS on
AUX[E;F]
visits at most once each vertex
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
such that there is a path of positive length or a cycle from some vertex

{c}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle\setminus C\langle E,F\rangle
to
{b}
. Moreover,
Algorithm PDFS visits a vertex

{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
such that

{b}.keeps_{F}=\mathrm{false}
.

We get the following result by the equivalence
\mathrm{(i)}\Leftrightarrow\mathrm{(iv)}
in Lemma
14.137
and by Lemma
14.138
.

Lemma 14.139
.

Let
\Sigma
be a signature such that

\Sigma_{k}\neq\emptyset
for some
k\geq 2
, and let

E
and
F
be GTESs over
\Sigma
such that GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
is total.

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
if and only if Algorithm PDFS on
AUX[E;F]
outputs true.

Lemma 14.140
.

Let
\Sigma
be a signature such that

\Sigma_{k}\neq\emptyset
for some
k\geq 2
, and let

E
and
F
be GTESs over
\Sigma
.
Assume that

R\langle E,F\rangle
is total.
Then we can decide in
O(n)
time whether

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

Proof.

We run Algorithm PDFS on
AUX[E;F]
in
O(n)
time by Lemma
14.134
. By Lemma
14.139
,
Algorithm PDFS on the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]
outputs the answer to the question whether

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.
∎

Lemma 14.141
.

Let
\Sigma
be a signature such that

\Sigma_{k}\neq\emptyset
for some
k\geq 2
, and let

E
and
F
be GTESs over
\Sigma
such that GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
is total.
Then we can decide in
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
time whether

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

Proof.

Recall that we have run Algorithm CAD on
\Sigma
,
E
, and
F
.
By Proposition
4.48
, we got
the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
for the GTESs
E
and
F
, and
for every vertex
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
the values of the attributes
{b}.equal_{E}
,
{b}.equal_{F}
,
{b}.keeps_{E}
, and
{b}.keeps_{F}
.
Then we run Algorithm PDFS on
AUX[E;F]
in
O(n)
time.
Algorithm PDFS on input
AUX[E;F]
outputs the answer to the question whether

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.
Thence the complexity of our entire computation is
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
.
∎

Example 14.142
.

We now continue Examples
3.22
,
7.74
,
10.110
, and
14.135
.
By the last lines of Example
10.110
, GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together do not simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
Accordingly Condition (iii) in Lemma
14.137
does not hold.
For this reason, by Lemma
14.137
,

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\not\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
. By the last line of Example,
14.135

Algorithm PDFS outputs false to the question whether
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.
As a consequence, our decision algorithm also outputs false to the question whether
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

Example 14.143
.

We now continue Examples
3.23
,
5.56
,
7.75
,
10.111
, and
14.136
.
By the last line of Example
10.111
,
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle

together simulate the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
.
Therefore Condition (iii) in Lemma
14.137
holds. Consequently, by Lemma
14.137
,

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
. By the last line of Example,
14.136

Algorithm PDFS outputs true to the question whether
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.
As a consequence, our decision algorithm also outputs true to the question whether
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

15
Main Case 4

In this main case,
E
and
F
are GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
such that

\Sigma
has a symbol of arity at least
2
,
and GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
are not total.
We assume that we have already run Algorithm CAD on
\Sigma
,
E
, and
F
, and have obtained all the output it produces, see Proposition
4.48
.
We show that
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}

if and only if

E\subseteq\Theta\langle F,E\rangle
or

F\subseteq\Theta\langle E,F\rangle
.
By direct inspection of the relations
\tau\langle E,F\rangle
and
\rho\langle F,E\rangle
, we decide whether
E\subseteq\Theta\langle F,E\rangle
.
Similarly, by direct inspection of the relations
\tau\langle F,E\rangle
and
\rho\langle E,F\rangle
, we decide whether
E\subseteq\Theta\langle F,E\rangle
.
Example

3.24
and its sequel Example
5.57
, furthermore, Example

3.25
and its sequels Examples
5.58
and
15.144

demonstrate this case.

Example 15.144
.

We continue Examples
3.25
and
5.58
. We recall that

f(\#,\mathsterling)\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}f(\$,\flat)
,

(f(\#,\mathsterling),f(\$,\flat))\not\in\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}
,   and

(f(\#,\mathsterling),f(\$,\flat))\not\in\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

The idea of the
(\Rightarrow)
direction of the proof of the next theorem comes from the proof of the above observation.

Theorem 15.145
.

Let
\Sigma
be a signature such that

\Sigma_{k}\neq\emptyset
for some
k\geq 2
. Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over
\Sigma
such that
GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
are not total.
Then

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}

if and only if

E\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
or

F\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}
.

Proof.

(\Rightarrow)
We proceed by contrapositive. Assume that

E\not\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
and

F\not\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}
.
That is, there exists

l_{1}\doteq r_{1}\in E
such that
(l_{1},r_{1})\not\in\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
and
there exists

l_{2}\doteq r_{2}\in F
such that
(l_{2},r_{2})\not\in\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}
.
Then
l_{1}\neq r_{1}
and
l_{2}\neq r_{2}
. Let
\sigma\in\Sigma_{k}
with
k\geq 2
, and by Lemma
4.43
, let
v\in T_{\Sigma}\setminus\bigcup W/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
,
and
z\in T_{\Sigma}\setminus\bigcup W/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}
.
Then

\sigma(\sigma(v,l_{1},\ldots,l_{1}),\sigma(z,l_{2},\ldots,l_{2}),v,\ldots,v)\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\sigma(\sigma(v,r_{1},\ldots,r_{1}),\sigma(z,r_{2},\ldots,r_{2}),v,\ldots,v)
.

We now show that

(\sigma(\sigma(v,l_{1},\ldots,l_{1}),\sigma(z,l_{2},\ldots,l_{2}),v,\ldots,v),\sigma(\sigma(v,r_{1},\ldots,r_{1}),\sigma(z,r_{2},\ldots,r_{2}),v,\ldots,v))\not\in\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
,

and hence we have

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\not\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

To this end, first we show that

(\sigma(\sigma(v,l_{1},\ldots,l_{1}),\sigma(z,l_{2},\ldots,l_{2}),v,\ldots,v),\sigma(\sigma(v,r_{1},\ldots,r_{1}),\sigma(z,r_{2},\ldots,r_{2}),v,\ldots,v))\not\in\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}
.

As
l_{1}\neq r_{1}
and
l_{2}\neq r_{2}
,

\sigma(\sigma(v,l_{1},\ldots,l_{1}),\sigma(z,l_{2},\ldots,l_{2}),v,\ldots,v)\neq\sigma(\sigma(v,r_{1},\ldots,r_{1}),\sigma(z,r_{2},\ldots,r_{2}),v,\ldots,v)
.

We now show that

(\sigma(\sigma(v,l_{1},\ldots,l_{1}),\sigma(z,l_{2},\ldots,l_{2}),v,\ldots,v),\sigma(\sigma(v,r_{1},\ldots,r_{1}),\sigma(z,r_{2},\ldots,r_{2}),v,\ldots,v))\not\in\mathop{\leftrightarrow}_{E}^{+}
.

To this purpose we now show the following statement.

Claim 15.146
.

Let

\sigma(\sigma(v,l_{1},\ldots,l_{1}),\sigma(z,l_{2},\ldots,l_{2}),v,\ldots,v)=s_{0}\mathop{\leftrightarrow}_{[\alpha_{1},p_{1}\doteq q_{1}]}s_{1}\mathop{\leftrightarrow}_{[\alpha_{2},p_{2}\doteq q_{2}]}\cdots\mathop{\leftrightarrow}_{[\alpha_{n},p_{n}\doteq q_{n}]}s_{n}=t,\;n\geq 1.

(3)

be a sequence of
E
-rewrite steps.
Then for each
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,n\,\}
,
\alpha_{i}
is not a prefix of
11
.

Proof.

By induction on
n
, we show that for each
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,n\,\}
,
\alpha_{i}
is not a prefix of
11
.

Base Case:

n=1
.
By the definition of
v
,
v\not\in\bigcup W/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
, hence

v
is not a subterm of

\sigma(\sigma(v,l_{1},\ldots,l_{1}),\sigma(z,l_{2},\ldots,l_{2}),v,\ldots,v)|_{\alpha_{1}}
,

thus

\alpha_{1}
is not a prefix of
11
.

Induction Step:
Let
n\geq 2
and let us assume that we have shown the
statement for
n-1
. Then by the induction hypothesis, for each
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,n-1\,\}
,
\alpha_{i}
is not a prefix of
11
. Accordingly
we have
v=s_{0}|_{11}\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}s_{n}|_{11}
. Consequently, by

v\not\in\bigcup W/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
, we have
s_{n}|_{11}\not\in\bigcup W/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
.
Thus

s_{n}|_{11}
is not a subterm of
p_{n}\in W
. As a consequence,

\alpha_{n}
is not a prefix of
11
. Then the induction step is complete.
∎

Consider the sequence (
3
) of
E
rewrite steps.
By Claim
15.146
,
for each
i\in\{\,1,\ldots,n\,\}
,
\alpha_{i}
is not a prefix of

11
. Consequently
t=\sigma(\sigma(t_{1},t_{2},\ldots t_{k}),p_{1},\ldots,p_{k-1})
for some
p_{1},\ldots,p_{k-1}\in T_{\Sigma}
, and
for each
j\in\{\,2,\ldots,k\,\}
,

l_{1}\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}t_{j}
. Recall that
(l_{1},r_{1})\not\in\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}
. Therefore
for each
j\in\{\,2,\ldots,k\,\}
,
t_{j}\neq r_{1}
. Hence

t\neq\sigma(\sigma(v,r_{1},\ldots,r_{1}),\sigma(z,r_{2},\ldots,r_{2}),v,\ldots,v)
.

Then

(\sigma(\sigma(v,l_{1},\ldots,l_{1}),\sigma(z,l_{2},\ldots,l_{2}),v,\ldots,v),\sigma(\sigma(v,r_{1},\ldots,r_{1}),\sigma(z,r_{2},\ldots,r_{2}),v,\ldots,v))\not\in\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}
.

We can show similarly that

(\sigma(\sigma(v,l_{1},\ldots,l_{1}),\sigma(z,l_{2},\ldots,l_{2}),v,\ldots,v)),\sigma(\sigma(v,r_{1},\ldots,r_{1}),\sigma(z,r_{2},\ldots,r_{2}),v,\ldots,v))\not\in\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

Consequently,

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\not\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

(\Leftarrow)

If
E\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
, then
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
, and hence
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.
If
F\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}
,
then
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}
, and hence
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.
∎

Consequence 15.147
.

Let
\Sigma
be a signature such that

\Sigma_{k}\neq\emptyset
for some
k\geq 2
. Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over
\Sigma
such that

R\langle E,F\rangle
and

R\langle F,E\rangle
are not total.
Then

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}

if and only if

E\subseteq\Theta\langle F,E\rangle
or

F\subseteq\Theta\langle E,F\rangle
.

Proof.

By Definition
2.10
, we have

\Theta\langle F,E\rangle=\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}\cap W\times W
and

\Theta\langle E,F\rangle=\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cap W\times W
.
Hence Theorem
15.145
implies our consequence.
∎

Lemma 15.148
.

Let
\Sigma
be a signature such that

\Sigma_{k}\neq\emptyset
for some
k\geq 2
, and let

E
and
F
be GTESs over
\Sigma
such that

R\langle E,F\rangle
and

R\langle F,E\rangle
are not total.
We can decide in
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
time whether
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

Proof.

By Consequence
15.147
, we proceed as follows.
Recall that we have run Algorithm CAD on
\Sigma
,
E
, and
F
.
By Proposition
4.48
, we got
the relations
\rho\langle E,F\rangle
,
\rho\langle F,E\rangle
, and
\rho\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
in
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
time.

First we decide whether
E\subseteq\Theta\langle F,E\rangle
.
By Proposition
4.26
,

E\subseteq\Theta\langle F,E\rangle
if and only if

for each
l\doteq r
in
E
,
(l,r)\in\Theta\langle F,E\rangle

if and only if

for each
(u,v)\in\tau\langle E,F\rangle
,

u/_{\rho\langle F,E\rangle}=v/_{\rho\langle F,E\rangle}
if and only if

\mathrm{FIND}_{\rho\langle F,E\rangle}(u)=\mathrm{FIND}_{\rho\langle F,E\rangle}(v)
.

We decide whether
for each
(u,v)\in\tau\langle E,F\rangle
,
\mathrm{FIND}_{\rho\langle F,E\rangle}(u)=\mathrm{FIND}_{\rho\langle F,E\rangle}(v)
.
If so,
E\subseteq\Theta\langle F,E\rangle
, otherwise
E\not\subseteq\Theta\langle F,E\rangle
.

Second, similarly to above, we decide whether
F\subseteq\Theta\langle E,F\rangle
.

By Consequence
15.147

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}

if and only if

E\subseteq\Theta\langle F,E\rangle
or

F\subseteq\Theta\langle E,F\rangle
.

We call above the functions
\mathrm{FIND}_{\rho\langle F,E\rangle}
and
\mathrm{FIND}_{\rho\langle E,F\rangle}
a total of
at most
2n
times,
where
n=\mathrm{size}(E)+\mathrm{size}(F)
.
These callings, by the main
result of
[
3
]
,
require
O(n)
time.
As a result, the time complexity of our entire computation is
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
.
∎

16
Main Result

We now sum up our decidability and complexity results.

Theorem 16.149
.

Let
E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
.
It is decidable in
O(n^{2})

time whether
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
, where
n=\mathrm{size}(E)+\mathrm{size}(F)
.

Proof.

We run Algorithm CAD on
\Sigma
,
E
, and
F
.
By Proposition
4.48
, we get the following output in
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
time:

1.

the relations
\rho\langle E,F\rangle
,
\rho\langle F,E\rangle
, and
\rho\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
;

2.

the set of nullary symbols
C\langle E,F\rangle
,
C\langle F,E\rangle
, and

C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
;

3.

INC(E\cup F,E)
stored in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT1
;

4.

INC(E\cup F,F)
stored in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT2
;

5.

NUM(E\cup F,E)
stored in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT3
;

6.

NUM(E\cup F,F)
stored in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT4
;

7.

the GTRS
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
stored in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT5
;
each node in
RBT5
contains a search key which is a rule in
R\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, and satellite data
counter_{E}
and
counter_{F}
;

8.

BSTEP\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
stored in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT6
;
for every ordered pair
({b},{c})\in BSTEP\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
, the search key is the first component
{b}
, the second component,
{c}
is satellite data;

9.

the GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
stored in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT7
;

10.

the GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
stored in a red-black tree saved in a variable
RBT8
;

11.

the auxiliary dpwpa
AUX[E;F]=(C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle,A[E;F])
for the GTESs
E
and
F
, and
for every vertex
{b}\in C\langle E\cup F,\emptyset\rangle
,
the values of the attributes
{b}.equal_{E}
,
{b}.equal_{F}
,
{b}.keeps_{E}
, and
{b}.keeps_{F}
.

By Lemma
4.50
, we decide in
O(n)
time whether

R\langle E,F\rangle
is total, and whether

R\langle F,E\rangle
is total.

1.

If both of them are total, then we proceed as in main Case 2, see Lemma
13.130
.

2.

Otherwise,

(a)

if
\Sigma
is a unary alphabet, then we proceed as in Main Case 1, see Lemma
12.123
.

(b)

If
\Sigma_{k}\neq\emptyset
for some
k\geq 2
, and at least one of
R\langle E,F\rangle

and
R\langle F,E\rangle
is total, then we proceed as in Main Case 3, see Lemma
14.141
.

(c)

If
\Sigma
has a symbol of arity at least
2
,
and GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle
and GTRS
R\langle F,E\rangle
are not total, then we proceed as in Main Case 4,
see Lemma
15.148
. ∎

By Theorem
16.149
and Proposition
9.92
, we have the following consequence.

Consequence 16.150
.

Let

E
and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
.
Then we can decide in
O(n^{2})
time whether
there exists a GTES
H
over
\Sigma
such that
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{H}=\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
, where
n=\mathrm{size}(E)+\mathrm{size}(F)
.
If the answer is
“yes”
, then
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
is a congruence on the ground term algebra
{\cal T}(\Sigma)
and

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}=\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
. Otherwise

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
is not a congruence on the ground term algebra
{\cal T}(\Sigma)
.

Proof.

By Theorem
16.149
, we decide in
O(n^{2})

time whether

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.
By Proposition
9.92
,
if the answer is
“yes”
, then there exists a GTES
H
over
\Sigma
such that
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{H}=\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
, and

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}=\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
;
otherwise

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
is not a congruence on the ground term algebra
{\cal T}(\Sigma)
.
∎

17
Conclusion

We showed that for any
GTESs
E
and
F
over a signature
\Sigma
,
the following four statements are equivalent:

1.

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}\subseteq\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

2.

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E\cup F}=\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

3.

There exists a GTES
H
over
\Sigma
such that
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{H}=\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
.

4.

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
is a congruence on the ground term algebra
{\cal T}(\Sigma)
.

We showed that we can decide in

O(n^{2})

time for given GTESs

E
and
F
over a signature
\Sigma
whether Statement 1 above holds. Here
n=\mathrm{size}(E)+\mathrm{size}(F)
.
We can construct the GTES
E\cup F
in
O(n^{2})
time.

We used the fast ground completion algorithm introducing additional constants
of Fülöp and Vágvölgyi
[
5
]
,
which, given a GTESs
E
and
F
over a signature
\Sigma

produces in
O(n\,\mathrm{log}\,n)
time a finite set
C\langle E,F\rangle
of new constants
and
a reduced GTRS
R\langle E,F\rangle

over
\Sigma\cup C\langle E,F\rangle

such that
\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}=\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{R\langle E,F\rangle}\cap T_{\Sigma}\times T_{\Sigma}
.
Along the proof, we distinguished four main cases. Main Case 2 overlaps with Main Case 1 and Main Case 3, and is a subcase of the union of
Main Case 1 and Main Case 3.

Given GTES
E
and GTES
F
, for every
p\in W
, we can construct the deterministic bottom-up tree automata

{\cal A}
,
{\cal B}
, and
{\cal C}
such that

{\cal A}
recognizes
p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}
,
{\cal B}
recognizes
p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}
, and

{\cal C}
recognizes
p/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup F}
, see Proposition
2.11
.
Thence, in Main Cases 1, 2, and 3, we can reduce our decidability
problem to deciding the equivalence of two deterministic bottom-up tree automata.
Champavère et al
[
10
]

presented an algorithm for deciding inclusion between two tree languages recognized by given bottom-up
tree automata. Applying their algorithm to our decision problem in Main Cases 1, 2, and 3 yields an alternative decision algorithm, which
is more sophisticated than ours, and in general takes more time than ours, see Lemmas
12.113
,
13.126
, and

14.131
.

We raise the following more general problem:

Problem 17.151
.

Let
D
,
E
, and
F
be GTESs over a signature
\Sigma
.
Can we decide whether

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{D\cup E\cup F}=\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{D}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
?
If we can, then what is the time complexity of our decision algorithm?

One might think that we can generalize our constructions and decidability results in a straightforward way. However, the following example shows that this belief is not justified.

Example 17.152
.

Consider the unary signature

\Sigma=\Sigma_{0}=\{\,\#,\;\$,\;\mathsterling\,\}
. Let

D=\{\,\#\doteq\$\,\}
,

E=\{\,\#\doteq\mathsterling\,\}
,

F=\{\,\$\doteq\mathsterling\,\}
,

H=D\cup E\cup F
.

Then

1.

ST\langle H\rangle=\Sigma_{0}
,

2.

\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{H}=\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{D}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}\cup\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}
,

3.

\#/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{D}}\subset\#/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{H}}
,
\#/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}\subset\#/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{H}}
,
\#/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}\subset\#/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{H}}
,

4.

\#/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{D}},\;\#/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}},\;\#/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}
are pairwise incomparable for the inclusion relation, and

5.

for
each
t\in ST\langle H\rangle
,

t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{D}}\subset t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{H}}
,
t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{E}}\subset t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{H}}
,   and
t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{F}}\subset t/_{\mathop{\leftrightarrow}^{*}_{H}}
.

Then the generalization of Condition (ii) in Lemma
12.122
does not hold.
